
### Paper [1]
Title: Interpretable machine learning of formation energies and band gaps in hybrid perovskites through SHAP analysis and SISSO modelling
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09944
Authors: Christopher Xia, Hao Zhang, Qingyuan Jin
Abstract: Accurate and interpretable property prediction is essential for exploring hybrid organic-inorganic perovskites. Here, we combine machine-learning screening with SHAP analysis and SISSO descriptor discovery. Using 4,456 compounds, optimized XGBoost models achieved test-set $R^2$ values of 0.99 for formation energy and 0.93 for band gap and were applied to over 180,000 candidates. Comparison across approximately 17,000 common compounds revealed broadly overlapping distributions from XGBoost and CGCNN but substantially lower predictions from UPET, suggesting potential limitations in its transferability to hybrid organic-inorganic perovskites. SHAP analysis and correlation-based pruning retained eight primary features for each property, from which SISSO derived expressions comprising four nonlinear descriptors. These expressions couple molecular and inorganic properties for formation energy and emphasize relative atomic sizes and B-site characteristics for band gap. This framework supports efficient candidate prioritization and interpretable analysis of learned structures-property relationships in hybrid perovskites.

### Paper [2]
Title: Two-dimensional liquid-like order in a confined population of swimming Eudorina colonies
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09946
Authors: Nicholas Tufillaro
Abstract: Swimming microorganisms accumulate at boundaries, but the spatial statistics of the resulting layers are rarely measured. We report a population of colonial green algae, of order 71 $\mu$m across, that accumulates against the rigid upper wall of a closed 1-2 mm chamber and holds a two-dimensional non-contacting arrangement for about 105 min. The pair correlation function has a first shell at $r/\bar{D} = 1.73$, $g = 4.8$; a hard-disc null at the same area fraction reaches only $g = 2.1$, so the ordering is not excluded volume alone. Nearest-neighbour separation is $1.80 \pm 0.13$ diameters. The bond-orientational order parameter is $\langle|\psi_6|\rangle = 0.456$ against 0.385 for the null, only 36% of interior colonies are six-coordinated, and the orientational correlation $g_6(r)$ decays over 1.5 diameters with $\eta_6 = 1.31$ against the hexatic bound of 1/4: the arrangement is a two-dimensional liquid, not a crystal or a hexatic. Colonies execute a persistent random walk with reorientation time 1.4-1.7 s at Reynolds number $\sim 9\times 10^{-4}$. Breakup begins at 127 min. Thermal convection is excluded on Rayleigh-number grounds. Caveats: the length calibration is nominal rather than metrological, and this is a single population observed once. All data and code are deposited.

### Paper [3]
Title: Interception Focusing of Colloid Transport in Granular Porous Media
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09947
Authors: William P. Johnson (University of Utah, Geology and Geophysics, Salt Lake City, Utah, USA), Noah G. Willis (University of Utah, Geology and Geophysics, Salt Lake City, Utah, USA), Luis A. Ullauri (University of Utah, Geology and Geophysics, Salt Lake City, Utah, USA), Bashar M. Al-Zghoul (University of California Davis, Civil and Environ. Eng., Davis, California, USA), Diogo Bolster (University of Notre Dame, Civil and Environmental Engineering and Earth Sciences, Notre Dame, Indiana, United States)
Abstract: Colloid transport and retention in environmental granular porous media underlie groundwater resource protection from, and remediation using, biological and non-biological nano- and micro-particles (colloids). During transport, colloids periodically intercept grain surfaces and translate along them at separation distances across several tens of nm corresponding to the secondary minimum between the opposing forces of longer-range of van der Waals (VDW) attraction and shorter-range electric double layer (EDL) repulsion. Relatively weak diffusive translation of colloids supports their continued translation in secondary minimum association until they are expelled to bulk fluid at rear flow stagnation zones on grain leeward sides. We herein show that this dynamic focuses colloids into a network of fluid streamlines (incomplete mixing lamellae) that enhances their subsequent likelihood of interception with downgradient grains, i.e., increases the so-called collector efficiency (typically denoted by Greek letter eta). Interception focusing of colloids in granular porous media explains well-observed outcomes of colloid transport under environmental conditions, including nonmonotonic nonexponential profiles of retained colloids and the non-Markovian nature of extended tailing of low colloid concentrations following initial elution. Interception focusing is a fundamental process that distinguishes colloids from solutes and renders inapplicable to colloids the assumption that is often invoked for solutes of complete pore scale mixing in granular porous media.

### Paper [4]
Title: Rapid Antigen Detection Tests Empowered by Cholesteric Spherical Reflectors for Expanded Capabilities
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09948
Authors: Chih-Chieh Chien, Yan-Song Zhang, Jean-Fran\c{c}ois Conrotte, Carole L. Linster, Sivashankar Krishnamoorthy, Jan P. F. Lagerwall
Abstract: While Rapid Antigen Detection Tests (RADTs) have proved their value in point-of-care detection of respiratory diseases, their poor-contrast readout limits their use at early stages of infection where viral concentrations are low, and most RADTs test only for one disease, leaving a sick patient ignorant in case of a negative result. We explore the potential of Cholesteric Spherical Reflectors (CSRs) as enhanced optical labeling probes for RADTs, leveraging their circularly polarized reflections to maximize contrast by background subtraction and thereby lower the analyte concentration required for detection. Moreover, we demonstrate how the ease in tuning the CSR reflection color and the freedom to choose between right- and left-handed circularly polarized reflection allow us to design CSRs to distinguish optically between multiple diseases, eventually enabling RADTs capable of screening for a set of multiple diseases in a single test. We develop a method for functionalizing CSRs with appropriate antibodies and we propose a new type of RADT specifically designed for use with CSRs as detection label, demonstrating that the comparatively large size of CSRs (tens of microns in diameter) does not prevent viral analytes to tether them to an antibody-coated test surface. The strength of the optical CSR signal quantitatively reflects the antigen concentration.

### Paper [5]
Title: Manufacturing Complex Structures Through Interfacial Self-Organization
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09950
Authors: Jacob Garrett, Abhinit Sati, Edward Chien, Emily Whiting, Valeri Frumkin
Abstract: Conventional manufacturing constructs three-dimensional geometries through localized material deposition, removal, or shaping, with fabrication effort greatly increasing with geometric complexity. Here, we introduce a manufacturing approach in which target geometry is encoded into sparse wireframe scaffolds that guide liquid self-organization through interfacial forces. We apply a computational framework that approximates arbitrary target geometries using constant-mean-curvature surface domains and extracts their boundaries to construct the scaffolds. Under neutral buoyancy conditions, curable liquids injected into these scaffolds self-organize into the prescribed geometries, which are subsequently solidified into mechanically isotropic structures with optically smooth surfaces. We demonstrate the fabrication of complex anatomical models and topology-optimized structures featuring intricate branching geometries, internal cavities, and integrated materials. This approach opens new possibilities for scalable fabrication of complex geometric designs.

### Paper [6]
Title: Hierarchical Design of Ambidirectional LCE Sheets for Three-State Gaussian Shape Morphs
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09952
Authors: Atalaya Milan Wilborn, Gurminder K. Paink, Raphael Kay, Patryk Wasik, Friedrich Stricker, Jacopo Movilli, Shucong Li, Joanna Aizenberg
Abstract: Modulation of surface curvature is a commonly exploited strategy to achieve adaptability within natural and synthetic systems where structure governs function. While Gaussian curvature of inextensible surfaces is fixed, surfaces made of extensible materials can adapts their Gaussian curvature under strain, unlocking capabilities such as locomotion, dynamic coloration, and tunable stiffness. This feature is commonly exploited to develop soft actuators capable of complex functionalities; however, traversing the entire spectrum of Gaussian curvature was previously difficult to achieve without the use of multi-material constructs or spatially varied stimulus fields. In this work, we present a strategy for generating a diverse range of dynamic Gaussian shape morphs using multiphase liquid crystal elastomers (LCEs) as ambidirectionally deforming building blocks. Taking advantage of the materials sequential contraction and elongation during phase transitions, we design and investigate the deformation response of radially aligned flat LCE sheets which consecutively morph into negatively and positively curved flat surfaces in response to monotonically increasing temperature. We then investigate the influence of mesoscale asymmetry and macroscale geometry on curvature magnitude and bending symmetry, thereby demonstrating that a single form factor can be programmed to undergo a rich library of deformation behaviors. Finally, inspired by the strategy of cephalopods to couple mechanical deformability to dynamic optical properties, we fabricate a structurally colored LCE sheet which couples its dynamic Gaussian curvature to a dynamic color-changing response. This approach offers a route toward adaptive structures capable of multistate shape reconfiguration for applications in soft robotics, deployable systems, and responsive surfaces.

### Paper [7]
Title: A liquid implant that grows into a stable scaffold in situ
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09961
Authors: Wenqiang Li, Qichen Peng, Fuhong Wang, Zhentan Quan, Shengxiang Cai, Zhuoming Zhou, Jin Zhang, Ting Liu, Xuanping Xie, Lin Zhu, Bo Wang, Shuang Wang, Yuan An, Yingtong Zhou, Zirong Liu, Haohui Li, Songshen Li, Ben Wang, Xinjian Yang, Quanshui Zheng, Hongzhang Wang
Abstract: Endovascular embolization requires materials that can pass through narrow microcatheters yet form stable, conformal occlusions within irregular vascular lesions, but reconciling these competing requirements remains challenging. Here we report a wirelessly activated liquid precursor whose final architecture is constructed only after target localization. A gallium-based liquid metal containing iron microparticles and gas microcavities remains deformable, magnetically navigable, and retrievable before activation. Wireless induction heating activates the internal reactive microenvironment, coupling gas-driven microcavity growth with oxidative mineralization of newly generated gallium interfaces to form a porous, self-supporting scaffold. In aneurysm and arteriovenous malformation models, activation enabled durable occlusion, aneurysm-neck healing, and six-month stability in a porcine aneurysm model. These findings establish post-delivery construction as a strategy for creating stable therapeutic implants from minimally invasive liquid precursors.

### Paper [8]
Title: Topology and Information in Regularized Transport on Graphs
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09968
Authors: Shintaro Yoshizawa
Abstract: We study how two geometries of mass on a finite graph interact: the information geometry of the generalized entropy $F_q$ used for regularized transport, and the arithmetic of divisors governed by the Riemann--Roch theorem of Baker and Norine and by the Jacobian. Because the correction term of $F_q$ is affine, $F_q$-regularization at strength $\gamma$ is Tsallis regularization at temperature $\gamma/q$. Over the reals, chip-firing connects all configurations of equal degree, so the Jacobian enters transport only through integrality. We introduce the transport defect, the worst-case cost of steering $d$ chips towards a target by chip-firing, and prove $1/d\le\delta_{\TV}(D)\le(d-r(D))/d=(g-1-r(K-D))/d\le\min(1,g/d)$ whenever $g\ge1$; vertex multiplicities give a lower bound and an equality criterion. A regularized topological gap $\Gamma_{q,\gamma}(D,E)$ tends to the chip-move defect as $\gamma\to0$, lies in an explicit window, has a high-temperature slope given by Tsallis coupling entropies, and admits a positive regime if and only if $g\ge1$. Dual potentials certify exactly the convex relaxation of a linear system; the remainder is an integrality gap. Zeros in optimal plans occur only for $q>1$, by a mechanism different from the sign change of $\Gamma$. Exact computations on $1784$ divisor classes find the rank bound $(d-r(D))/d$ attained in $1769$ of them, an observed frequency. Experiments on honeycomb and Erd\H{o}s--R\'enyi networks with $10^2$ to $10^5$ nodes test the statements on regularized plans, not $\Gamma$; over $10^3\le n\le10^5$ their transport costs are consistent with growth like $n^{1/2}$ and $\log n$, respectively.

### Paper [9]
Title: Enhancement of the magnetorheological effect using discontinuous shear thickening with a mixture of magnetic and non magnetic particles
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09970
Authors: Georges Bossis (INPHYNI), Yan Grasselli (INPHYNI), Olga Volkova (INPHYNI)
Abstract: The phenomenon of discontinuous shear thickening (DST) is observed in suspensions of solid particles with a very high-volume fraction. Using a suspension of magnetic particles at high-volume fraction we have, for the first time, demonstrated experimentally the presence of a frictional network of particles by a simultaneous measurement of the electrical resistance of the suspension and of the rheological curve. An obstacle for the applications of MRS remains the sedimentation and aggregation linked to the high density of the iron microparticles. We have recently found that a small fraction of ferromagnetic particles within a concentrated suspension of calcium carbonate particles is not only sufficient to trigger the transition by application of a magnetic field but also optimum for the amplitude of the jump in viscosity observed in DST. We shall explain this result from stress transmission between the magnetic particles and nonmagnetic ones, both inserted in the percolated network of particles. We will also highlight the role of the polyelectrolyte adsorbed on the surface of the particles in the DST transition through the balance between the repulsive entropic force between two layers of polymer adsorbed on different particles and the magnetic attraction force.

### Paper [10]
Title: Conductance scaling in nanopore arrays: disentangling roles of pore arrangement and reservoir geometry
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09991
Authors: Jacob Bair, Zachery Gottshall, Matthias Kuehne
Abstract: The scaling of electrical conductance with the number of nanopores, $N$, in an array is of fundamental importance for applications in membrane separation and ion transport across biological membranes, and has been the subject of longstanding disagreement in the field. Combining finite element calculations with theory, we resolve this disagreement for pores with negligible surface charge by showing that two previously conflicting predictions are each correct in their own limiting reservoir geometry: cuboidal reservoirs commensurate with the interpore spacing, for which the conductance scales linearly with $N$, and infinite reservoirs, for which the scaling is sublinear in $N$ for closely spaced pores. We show the transition between these limits with increasing reservoir size and, for infinite reservoirs, we show that a simple analytical model from electrical contact theory is mathematically equivalent to an existing point-charge capacitance model for nanopore arrays and validate its applicability against finite element and method-of-moments calculations. Using this model, we derive a characteristic coupling length, $\ell_\mathrm{c,95}$, the interpore spacing at which the array conductance reaches $95\%$ of that of the same number of independent pores. $\ell_\mathrm{c,95}$ scales linearly with pore diameter $d$, and grows with $N$ as $\sim\log N$ for linear arrays and $\sim\sqrt{N}$ for square arrays, so that pore coupling persists to surprisingly large separations in large arrays. Our work provides a general framework for predicting nanopore array conductance as a function of pore arrangement and reservoir geometry, of importance to nanofluidics research and the targeted design of functional devices.

### Paper [11]
Title: Memory flows: geometry and dynamics of sequential retrieval in input-driven Hopfield networks
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09995
Authors: Simone Betteti, Matteo Martin, Morten Gram Pedersen, Sandro Zampieri, Giacomo Baggio
Abstract: Associative memory models classically describe retrieval as convergence to stored patterns, but the theory of sequential retrieval remains comparatively limited. We develop a two-timescale input-driven Hopfield network in which fast associative retrieval is modulated by a slow feedback variable evolving over a prescribed memory-transition graph. This yields autonomous, graph-constrained transitions while preserving analytical tractability. Using slow-fast and geometric singular perturbation theory, we characterize memory fixation via memory manifolds and identify the geometric regions that govern stability loss and escape from retrieved memories. We further derive reduced slow dynamics and explicit criteria for transition onset, self-sustained retrieval, and collapse, expressed through escape times, gain thresholds, and fixed points. Together, these results provide a tractable framework for analyzing feedback-driven sequential retrieval in Hopfield networks.

### Paper [12]
Title: A transversely isotropic constitutive model for semisoft elasticity in main-chain liquid crystal elastomers
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10060
Authors: Donghao Li, Xin Yi, Fan Feng
Abstract: Monodomain main-chain liquid crystal elastomers exhibit pronounced stiffness anisotropy and broad semisoft plateaus following finite onset strains. At fixed chain anisotropy, the neo-classical model predicts equal principal Young's moduli, while its transversely isotropic extension with a constant semisoft modulus constrains the relation between reorientation onset and subsequent stress growth. We develop a finite-strain, isothermal elastic model combining transversely isotropic network elasticity with a deformation-dependent quadratic semisoft penalty. Within a weak-fluctuation approximation, a reduced statistical closure based on assumed linear contraction of effective local anisotropy fluctuations at fixed mean anisotropy yields an exponential transition from an initial to a residual semisoft modulus. The network energy preserves ideal soft modes, and the formulation recovers the de Gennes strain-director coupling in the infinitesimal limit. Analytical relations for perpendicular tension show that the rotating-stage stress depends on both the current semisoft modulus and its derivative with respect to the first deformation invariant. This permits a finite onset followed by a substantially flatter rotating response than in the constant-modulus limit. Oblique tension couples director rotation, shear and lateral contraction. Comparisons with orientation-dependent measurements using a common parameter set capture the principal stiffness contrast and broad perpendicular plateau, with discrepancies remaining in oblique stresses and intermediate-angle initial moduli.

### Paper [13]
Title: Roadmap on Nanoscale Superconductivity for Quantum Technologies
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10076
Authors: Oleksandr Dobrovolskiy, Hermann Suderow, Francesco Tafuri, Annica M. Black-Schaffer, Jose L. Lado, Asle Sudb{\o}, Daniela Stornaioulo, Chuan Li, Anna E. B\"ohmer, Lan Maria Tran, Andrzej J. Zaleski, Adrian Crisan, Massimiliano Polichetti, Armando Galluzzi, Ali Gencer, Bernd Aichner, Neven Bari\v{s}i\'c, Wolfgang Lang, Tomas Samuely, Martin Gmitra, Tristan Cren, Mateo Calandra, Peter Samuely, Jeroen Custers, Rosa C\'ordoba, Vladimir M. Fomin, Nicola Poccia, Pavol Szab\'o, Fabrizio Porrati, Gleb Kakazei, Jan Aarts, Jason Robinson, Javier E. Villegas, Matthias Althammer, Hans Huebl, Akashdeep Kamra, Mathias Weiler, J. Hugo Dil, Daniil Evtushinsky, Beena Kalisky, Yonathan Anahory, Simon Bending, Peter Liljeroth, Abdou Hassanien, Isabel Guillam\'on, Edwin Herrera, Alejandro V. Silhanek, Joris Van de Vondel, Anna Palau, Ilya Charaev, Maria Sidorova, Floriana Lombardi, Thilo Bauch, Cheryl Feuillet-Palma, Vasily Stolyarov, Dimitri Roditchev, Vladimir M. Krasnov, Benedikt Hampel, Mar\'ia Jos\'e Mart\'inez-P\'erez, Javier Ses\'e, Dieter Koelle, Stefano Poletto, Alessandro Bruno, Davide Massarotti
Abstract: In 2025, the Year of Quantum Science and Technology, we celebrate a century of quantum mechanics, witnessing a surge in activities that illuminate its inherent strangeness and drive technological innovation. Superconductivity, discovered 114 years ago, stands as a prime example, offering direct and compelling evidence of macroscopic quantum phenomena. Beyond its ability to conduct immense currents without loss, superconductivity reveals the quantum realm operating on a scale we can directly observe and manipulate. The macroscopic quantum coherence, where an ensemble of particles is described by a single wave function, leads to remarkable consequences: dissipation-less current and flux quantization - the basic properties exploited in superconducting quantum circuit fabrication. This Roadmap has been inspired by intensive discussions and collaborations emerging from the European COST Action CA21144 (SuperQuMap - Superconducting Nanodevices and Quantum Materials for Coherent Manipulation). The roadmap outlines the network's concrete activities, driving advancements in superconductor-based quantum technologies and charting future directions. Spanning fundamental research to practical applications, the roadmap incorporates insights from industry partners developing quantum computation. It begins by exploring quantum materials, highlighting how topology and electronic correlations could catalyze a quantum leap in technology. We then delve into manipulating the superconducting phase, leveraging advancements in magnetism, 3D fabrication, and tunable correlations. Further, we showcase the advanced microscopy techniques - such as angle-resolved photoemission spectroscopy and scanning probes - used to visualize quantum behavior. Finally, and crucially, we detail the quantum devices developed within the network, and their transformative impact on modern quantum computing approaches.

### Paper [14]
Title: Exceptional Topological Bound States in the Continuum
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10077
Authors: Luohong Liu, Qicheng Zhang, Shuaishuai Tong, Chunyin Qiu
Abstract: Topological phases, exceptional points, and bound states in the continuum (BICs) are three key concepts in condensed matter and classical wave physics. Their pairwise combinations have yielded a rich variety of intriguing singular modes, but the triple merger--exceptional topological BICs (ET-BICs)--has yet to be explored. In this Letter, we theoretically propose and experimentally demonstrate a universal approach for constructing such elusive exceptional structures. The system consists of four coupled topological chains: y-mirror stacking generates pairs of topological BICs, while z-stacking with biased loss yields a generalized mirror symmetry under which each pair--separated in different subspaces until their collapse--merges into an ET-BIC at a critical loss. Experimentally, using an acoustic platform with electrically tunable loss, we not only directly observe the coalescence of eigenenergies but also the coalescence of eigenstates, offering a complete verification of the ET-BIC formation. The triply synergistic ET-BICs may pave the way for wave devices that simultaneously demand robustness, sensitivity, and BIC confinement.

### Paper [15]
Title: Pelletization Pressure Engineering of the Superconducting Properties of Fluorine-Doped PrFeAsO
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10083
Authors: Jayant Kumar, Konrad Kwatek, Tatiana Zajarniuk, Taras Palasyuk, Yaroslaw Konopelnyk, Tomasz Cetner, Cezariusz Jastrz\k{e}bski, Shiv J. Singh
Abstract: We investigate the influence of the pelletization pressure on the structural, microstructural, and superconducting properties of fluorine-doped PrFeAsO0.7F0.3 (Pr1111). Precursor powders are pressed into pellets at pressures ranging from 174 to 1131 MPa, after which the applied pressure is released and all samples undergo an identical two-step conventional synthesis process at ambient pressure (CSP-AP). All samples predominantly exhibit the tetragonal P4/nmm structure, and the lattice parameter a and unit-cell volume V decrease with increasing pelletization pressure up to an intermediate-pressure range (~900 MPa) before increasing at higher pressures. The actual fluorine-content derived from energy-dispersive X-ray spectroscopy (EDX) also increase with pelletization pressure, while Raman spectra show only modest variations in the characteristic vibrational modes associated with Fe and As. The superconducting response exhibits a non-monotonic dependence on the pelletization pressure. Among the investigated samples, the sample pelletized at 870 MPa exhibits the most favorable overall superconducting properties, with a superconducting onset temperature of 44.2 K, ~5 K higher than that of the lowest-pressure sample. It also displays a narrower superconducting transition, the highest relative sample density, a favorable effective magnetic critical current density at high fields, and a relatively large effective thermally activated flux-flow activation energy. At higher pelletization pressures, the superconducting response deteriorates, coinciding with increases in the lattice parameters, increased secondary-phase contributions, and reduced relative density. These findings establish the pelletization pressure as a relevant processing parameter for modifying the structural, microstructural and superconducting properties of Pr1111 during CSP-AP.

### Paper [16]
Title: Superconductivity in the Metallic Wigner Crystals of Rhombohedral Graphene: Possible Realization of an Electronic Supersolid
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10117
Authors: F\'elix Desrochers, Mark R. Hirsbrunner, Yong Baek Kim, Ashvin Vishwanath
Abstract: Recent experiments on rhombohedral multilayer graphene have reported metallic Wigner crystals (MWCs), in which spontaneously broken translational symmetry coexists with mobile charge carriers. Their proximity to time-reversal-symmetry-breaking superconductivity raises the intriguing possibility that collective excitations of the electron crystal mediate pairing among the residual itinerant carriers, giving rise to a superconducting Wigner crystal---an electronic analog of a supersolid. Here we investigate this possibility using a realistic model of electron-doped rhombohedral multilayer graphene with spin and valley polarization, whose low symmetry removes the usual Cooper instability. We use self-consistent and time-dependent Hartree-Fock theory to map the evolution of the self-doping fraction and characterize the collective-mode spectrum of the MWCs, followed by a multiband linearized Eliashberg analysis of pairing. Upon increasing density, we find an instability of the MWC, triggered by a weakly dispersing unoccupied band approaching the chemical potential. Pairing remains weak deep in the stable MWC. However, on approaching the instability, we identify a collective mode composed of interband electron-hole excitations that softens. We show that this mode drives pairing at experimentally relevant temperatures. The leading pairing instability is at finite momentum and combines Fermi surface states with states in the incipient band, making a Fermi surface or single-band pairing analysis inadequate. Our results establish collective-mode-mediated superconductivity in MWCs as a viable scenario for rhombohedral graphene and motivate experimental searches for coexisting electronic charge order in its superconducting phases.

### Paper [17]
Title: Energy-resolved current response and the crossover between two approximately linear-in-$T$ resistivity regimes in the infinite-$U$ Hubbard model
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10124
Authors: Shuo Liu, Aman Kumar, Yuhao Ma, Philip W. Phillips, Hitesh J. Changlani, B. Andrei Bernevig
Abstract: We study charge transport in the two-dimensional infinite-$U$ Hubbard model with hopping amplitude $t$ using full exact diagonalization (ED) on $N=16$- and $18$-site square clusters with a single doped hole. We first provide a proof, treating degeneracies explicitly, that a constant dc $f_x$ function, defined as the sum of the squared current matrix elements connecting eigenstates at a given energy to eigenstates of nearly the same energy, averaged over degenerate initial states, is necessary and sufficient for strictly linear-in-$T$ resistivity: $\rho_x(T)=\alpha T$ for all $T>0$ with a constant $\alpha$. The numerically extrapolated dc $f_x$ is not exactly constant, but it is approximately flat over a broad region in the interior of the many-body spectrum and varies strongly within a scale $\varepsilon_{f_x}$ of the spectral edges. This scale $\varepsilon_{f_x}$ is related to a characteristic temperature $T_{\mathrm{cr}}$, which quantifies the leading departure of the resistivity from high-temperature linearity upon cooling. Within the studied temperature range $T/t \geq 0.05$, our ED resistivity results reveal a gradual crossover between high- and low-temperature approximately linear-in-$T$ regimes, with characteristic crossover temperatures $T_{\mathrm{cr}}/t\approx0.414$ and $0.467$ for $N=16$ and $18$, respectively. We also find a broad specific-heat peak associated with this gradual crossover, at $T/t \approx 0.70$ and $0.68$ for $N=16$ and $18$, respectively.

### Paper [18]
Title: Mimicry of $\textit{d}$-wave Andreev Signatures by Interlayer Pairing
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10130
Authors: Zhongyi Zhang, Shengshan Qin, Chui-Zhen Chen, Hoi Chun Po, Jiangping Hu, Xianxin Wu
Abstract: The recent discovery of high-$T_{\rm c}$ superconductivity in bilayer nickelates has attracted enormous attention. Despite dominant theoretical studies pointing to interlayer pairing, the pairing symmetry is strongly debated due to conflicting evidence on nodeless bulk gaps and $d$-wave-like transport signatures. Here, we demonstrate that a nodeless interlayer pairing can naturally mimic $d$-wave Andreev signatures when the point contact is not evenly coupled to the two layers. The phenomenon can be understood by recognizing how a contact potential, which is odd under the layer-exchanging mirror symmetry, acts like an effective Zeeman field on the layer pseudospin. This pseudo field then binds in-gap Andreev states that sweep through the gap with the width and strength of the barrier. We further demonstrate in a realistic model for La$_3$Ni$_2$O$_7$ that the simulated subgap conductance for a top-layer barrier can reproduce the orientation dependent ZBCP usually interpreted as a fingerprint of $\textit{d}$-wave pairing. By revealing this mirror-selective Andreev response from an interlayer pairing state, our work offers a natural scenario to reconcile the conflicting experimental evidence in bilayer nickelates and advances our understanding of unconventional superconductivity.

### Paper [19]
Title: Controlled Oxidation of Tellurium Nanoparticles for Telecom Detection
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10135
Authors: Biman Jana
Abstract: Surface oxidation plays an important role in governing charge transport in semiconductor nanoparticle assemblies. Here, we investigate the optoelectronic properties of solution-processed tellurium (Te) nanoparticle ensembles prepared through gradual ambient-air exposure. Structural and spectroscopic analyses reveal interconnected elemental Te nanoparticles with coexisting alpha-TeO2 and hydroxyl-containing surface species, exhibiting a lower relative contribution of oxidized Te than previously investigated forced-air-oxidized ensembles. The resulting photodetectors demonstrate photoresponse at 405, 532, and 1550 nm, with maximum responsivities of 10.1, 14.4, and 8.5 mA/W, respectively. At 1550 nm, the device exhibits rise and fall times of 17.7 and 40.2 s, respectively, and an estimated shot-noise-limited specific detectivity of 1.3 * 10^6 Jones. These findings highlight the importance of controlling surface oxidation to balance interparticle charge transport and dark-current suppression, offering insights for the development of solution-processed Te-based photodetectors for short-wave infrared applications.

### Paper [20]
Title: Hidden memory above the Neel temperature programs antiferromagnetic order in Cr2O3
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10173
Authors: Josiah Keagy, Sumukh Mahesh, Wei-Cheng Liao, Jing Shi
Abstract: Control of antiferromagnetic order is central to magnetoelectric and antiferromagnetic spintronic devices. In the prototypical magnetoelectric antiferromagnet Cr2O3, domains are conventionally selected by simultaneous application of electric and magnetic fields during cooling through the Neel temperature (T_N) or at a fixed temperature below T_N. Here, we show that antiferromagnetic order can instead be programmed by electric-field conditioning entirely in the paramagnetic state above T_N. By measuring the magnetoelectric coefficient (alpha_zz) of bulk Cr2O3 crystals under different thermal and field histories, we find that a sufficiently large electric field above T_N establishes a reversible state that persists after the field is removed. Subsequent magnetic-field cooling with no applied electric field produces a single-domain state with the opposite Neel-vector orientation from conventional magnetoelectric field cooling. The electrically induced state can be reversibly written, erased, and allowed to relax at high temperatures, demonstrating a long-lived internal electric bias that governs domain selection. The observed behavior is consistent with a metastable surface-associated polar state that may generate this internal bias. These results reveal an unexpected coupling between electrically programmed memory and antiferromagnetic domain formation in Cr2O3.

### Paper [21]
Title: Optimizing one-dimensional lithographically defined nanowires in semiconducting heterostructures
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10177
Authors: Leah Tom, Yik Kheng Lee, Karina L. Hudson, Krittika Kumar, Jackson S. Smith, Alex R. Hamilton, S. N. Coppersmith, Jared H. Cole, Mark Friesen
Abstract: One-dimensional nanowires can host remarkable electronic properties and are central to studying quantum transport. Although heterostructure-defined confinement combined with gate-defined potentials can greatly reduce the effects of impurities as measured via electron mobilities, the realization and interpretation of novel effects at this scale are complicated by disorder. Experiments on lithographically defined one-dimensional nanowires therefore do not exhibit ideal behavior. Using theoretical and computational models, we predict that optimizing the geometry of split gates in semiconducting heterostructures will lead to higher-quality channels for transport measurements. Further, we show that this optimization is essential for resolving features of interest such as the Rashba-induced spin gap. This new approach to lithographically defined conduction channels provides better energy resolution and is less susceptible to disorder.

### Paper [22]
Title: Current-voltage characteristics of layered cuprate superconductors with intrinsic Josephson junctions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10183
Authors: T. B. Charikova, V. N. Neverov, M. R. Popov, S. D. Popov, N. G. Shelushinina
Abstract: A brief review of the results for the current-voltage characteristics study in highly anisotropic cuprate superconductors is presented, considering the existence of the intrinsic Josephson effect in multilayer HTSC compounds. We analyzed the observations of multi-branch current-voltage characteristics for current in the c-direction both for the hole-doped and electron-doped cuprate SCs wich conveniently viewed as a strack of intrinsic Josephson junctions, where the CuO2 layers serve as superconducting plates and the buffer layers as dielectric interlayers. It was shown that the tunneling characteristics of intrinsic Josephson junctions in optimally doped p- and n-type cuprates indicate a predominantly d-wave symmetry of the order parameter, demonstrating high conductivity in the subgap regime: U < 2delta/e. The analysis carried out confirms the theoretically predicted power-law dependences U ~ I^gamma with gamma = (0.33 - 0.50) in the hole-doped Bi- and Tl-compounds, which is typical for SCs with monotonic dx^2-y^2 - wave symmetry of the order parameter and with gamma = (0.70 - 0.76) for electron-doped SCs with a non-monotonic d-wave symmetry within the framework of the two-band/two-gap model.

### Paper [23]
Title: Temperature-Driven Evolution of Magnetization Dynamics in Cobalt-Doped Fe$_5$GeTe$_2$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10225
Authors: Sourabh Singh, Soumik Aon, Andrew F. May, Joost Ridderbos, Ramanathan Mahendiran, Wilfred G. van der Wiel
Abstract: Understanding magnetization dynamics in metallic van der Waals ferromagnets is essential to uncover their magnetic interactions and evaluate their potential for high-frequency spintronics applications. In this study, we investigate the temperature-dependent magnetization dynamics of a single crystal of cobalt-doped $Fe_{5}GeTe_{2}$ (CFGT) using broadband ferromagnetic resonance (FMR) spectroscopy. At high temperatures, the FMR response is dominated by a single broad resonance that shifts toward lower magnetic fields upon cooling. Fits to the frequency dependence of the resonance field and linewidth yield an effective $g$ factor of approximately 2.45 and an effective Gilbert damping parameter of approximately 0.15 near room temperature. The effective magnetic anisotropy evolves strongly with temperature, with an increasingly pronounced easy-plane contribution upon cooling toward 200 K. Below approximately 200 K, an additional resonance emerges and evolves into a distinct two-mode response that persists to the lowest measured temperature of 25 K. The two branches exhibit different temperature dependences of their resonance fields and linewidths, and the emergence of the additional mode is not accompanied by a comparably pronounced anomaly in the static magnetization. These results reveal a dynamical crossover in CFGT that is not readily apparent from bulk magnetometry and highlight the sensitivity of FMR to the magnetic behavior of chemically substituted van der Waals ferromagnets.

### Paper [24]
Title: A Transport-Constrained Gradient-Flow Description of Continuum Dislocation Dynamics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10232
Authors: Paria Behnoud, Anthony Rollett, Carlos Mora-Corral, Kaushik Dayal
Abstract: Gradient flow frameworks are fundamental to numerous models of defect and microstructure evolution. This work develops a gradient flow formulation for continuum dislocation dynamics, with the key distinction that the admissible rates are transport-constrained. Using the dislocation density and compatible slip fields as the primary state variables, their admissible rates are restricted by geometric conservation laws. The corresponding transport velocities are then selected variationally through a constrained Onsager principle. The resulting model has both a variational gradient flow structure and consistency with the geometric dislocation conservation statement; existing models of dislocation evolution typically have either one or the other feature.
  This thermodynamic structure provides a dislocation driving force that includes elastic stress contributions -- corresponding to the classical Peach--Koehler force -- as well as contributions from the core and nonconvex lattice energies. A simplified version of the model is used to study the energetics and dynamics of an opposite-sign dislocation pair. The lattice energy keeps the cores compact, and an external applied shear either assists annihilation or, above a threshold, separates the pair.

### Paper [25]
Title: On the nature of low-energy electronic states in twisted bilayer graphene: a first-principles study
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10253
Authors: Albert Zhu, Daniel Bennett, Daniel T. Larson, Mohammed M. Al Ezzi, Efstratios Manousakis, Efthimios Kaxiras
Abstract: The nature of low-energy electronic states in twisted bilayer graphene (TBG) near the magic angle has been extensively discussed in the literature on qualitative grounds, since these states play a key role in the construction of effective few-band models toward a many-body theory. Here, we report extensive and systematic first-principles calculations of TBG electronic states in the magic-angle range, using density functional theory with no further approximations. We construct moir\'e-scale localized Wannier states for the low-energy bands and develop an effective ten-band model for each $K$ valley. The Wannier orbitals contain both atomic and moir\'e-scale details and are centered near the high-symmetry stackings in the moir\'e cell. We calculate Coulomb and exchange integrals of these localized states, quantities that are the basic ingredients in many-body models. We also compare the values of these integrals to those obtained or assumed in previous works. By symmetrizing the ten-band Hamiltonians and basis orbitals, we provide insight into how the dominant hoppings between the flat-band orbitals evolve as a function of twist angle.

### Paper [26]
Title: Atomically abrupt sp-sp$^2$ all-carbon heterojunction preserving the electronic identity of constituent allotropes
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10272
Authors: Alice Cartoceti, Masoumeh Alihosseini, Gianni Conti, Eliecer Pelaez-Sifonte, Alessio Orbelli Biroli, Francesco Sedona, Paolo D'Agosta, Francesco Tumino, Andrea Li Bassi, Jorge Lobo-Checa, Carlo S. Casari, Simona Achilli
Abstract: Covalently integrating distinct carbon allotropes with atomic precision remains, to date, elusive, despite the clear advantage of combining complementary functionalities while preserving their electronic identities. Here, we realize an all-carbon lateral heterojunction combining ($sp^2$) armchair graphene nanoribbons (GNRs) and $sp$-$sp^2$ hydrogenated graphdiyne (hGDY) on Au(111) through Fe-mediated hierarchical on-surface synthesis. Low-temperature scanning tunnelling microscopy and spectroscopy combined with density-functional theory reveal an atomically abrupt (<0.5 nm), asymmetric type-I interface. Redistribution of an edge-localized GNR state onto the first hGDY unit demonstrates local electronic continuity, whereas their characteristic electronic structures re-emerge immediately beyond the interface. Calculations further indicate that heterojunction formation weakens the interaction of both constituents with the underlying Au(111) substrate, widening their gaps and reducing adsorption-induced interfacial polarization. These results establish lateral covalent integration as a route to engineer atomically defined electronic interfaces and substrate coupling in all-carbon nanostructures.

### Paper [27]
Title: Coherent Electrometer Using Hybridised Double Quantum Dot - Resonator States
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10274
Authors: Samuel Andersson, Pierre Glidic, Andrea Cicovic, Harald Havir, Sebastian Lehmann, Kimberly A. Dick, Ville F. Maisi
Abstract: Probing the change in conductance or reactance of a charge sensitive system using an rf cavity is a standard way of doing fast single-charge electrometry. In this article, we present a detector concept which is instead based on coherent interaction. We show that the coherent interaction between a microwave resonator and double quantum dot gives rise to a fast, highly sensitive electrometer. The coherent electrometer achieves a state-of-the-art readout speed, resolving two charge states with SNR of 11.7 in 16 $\mu$s, with a readout signal two orders of magnitude lower in power than previously required to reach this speed. In this way, heating of the sample becomes negligible. Thanks to its sensitivity and low dissipation, the coherent electrometer opens up the avenue for quantum limited charge detection with nanosecond speed and minimal back-action.

### Paper [28]
Title: Fabrication and Characterization of Flexible In2O3 Thin-Film Transistors on Mica Substrates
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10354
Authors: Hyeonjun Kong, Ping-Hsein Wu, Xian-Ye Jiang, Yasutaka Matsuo, Ying-Hao Chu, Hiromichi Ohta
Abstract: Flexible oxide-semiconductor thin-film transistors (TFTs) with high field-effect mobility (uFE > 70 cm^2/Vs) are required for next-generation flexible displays. Polycrystalline In2O3-based TFTs are promising candidates; however, conventional flexible substrates such as plastics are not suitable because their fabrication typically requires relatively high temperatures (~350 deg.C). In this study, we used mica as a flexible substrate to fabricate polycrystalline In2O3-based TFTs, motivated by previous demonstrations of crystalline oxide growth on mica. The resulting TFTs exhibited a uFE of approximately 75 cm^2/Vs. The devices maintained gate-modulated operation after 10^3 flex-out bending cycles at a 5 mm radius, whereas flex-in cycling caused early electrical failure, likely due to In2O3 film degradation. The TFTs also operated normally after exposure to 85 deg.C and 40% relative humidity for one day. These results demonstrate that mica substrates enable high-mobility, flex-out-compatible In2O3-based TFTs.

### Paper [29]
Title: Discovery of Kondo-Kramers Nodal Line Topological State in strongly correlated material CeAuAl$_3$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10368
Authors: Hari Bhandari, Kevin Allen, Kuan-Sen Li, Yuan Fang,  Arushi, Karthik Rao, Mounica Mahankali, Sanu Mishra, Jian-Xin Zhu, Christopher Lane, Qimiao Si, Emilia Morosan
Abstract: Kramers nodal lines (KNLs) are predicted in all achiral, non-centrosymmetric crystal structures, and yet there are very few experimental realizations of KNLs, only in weakly correlated systems. Especially, their realization in strongly correlated systems remains unexplored. Here we investigate KNL topology in the non-centrosymmetric, achiral Kondo lattice CeAuAl$_3$, using magnetotransport, thermodynamic measurements, density functional theory (DFT), and tight-binding toy model calculations. We find that CeAuAl$_3$ hosts KNLs in the $\mathit{\Gamma}\text{-}Z$ and $X\text{-}P$ directions, protected by time-reversal and mirror symmetries. Breaking of these crystalline symmetries, along with time-reversal symmetry via spin polarization, lifts the KNL degeneracy, which, in turn, generates Berry curvature resulting in an intrinsic anomalous Hall effect (AHE). More importantly, CeAuAl$_3$ shows large AHE not only below the magnetic ordering temperature $T_N$ but rather below the Kondo temperature. This underscores the role of the Kondo correlations in bringing the KNLs at the Fermi surface rather than at accidental energies, as would be expected without correlations. Our work identifies a new topological state, Kondo-Kramers Nodal Line, with CeAuAl$_3$ being the first compound explicitly identified to host this new correlated topological state, and demonstrating the expected AHE associated with it. More generally, our work outlines the pathway for finding Kondo-KNL systems in all compounds that combine achiral, non-centrosymmetric structure with Kondo correlations.

### Paper [30]
Title: Momentum-Resolved Kondo Hybridization of Nonsymmorphic Dirac-Derived Bands in CeAgBi$_2$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10417
Authors: Yang Luo, Hao Liu, Zhong-Tuo Fu, Qi-Yi Wu, Bo Chen, Zheng-Tai Liu, Mao Ye, Da-Wei Shen, Chen Zhang, Ying Zhou, Cun-Hui Lyu, Jin-Dong Bai, Zi-Jie Xu, Hai-Long Deng, Zhen-Xing Wu, Jun-Jie Wu, Sai-Chao Deng, Yu-Xia Duan, Jian-Qiao Meng
Abstract: How symmetry-constrained conduction states acquire heavy-quasiparticle character is a central question in correlated quantum materials. Combining angle-resolved photoemission spectroscopy, Ce $4d \rightarrow 4f$ resonant photoemission, and first-principles calculations, we investigate the nonsymmorphic Kondo lattice CeAgBi$_2$. We identify quasi-two-dimensional Bi-square-net-derived bands consistent with calculated Dirac crossings and nodal lines. Resonant photoemission resolves a nearly dispersionless Ce 4$f$ band near the Fermi level, establishing Kondo heavy-quasiparticle states. Its pronounced momentum-dependent spectral weight at intersections with the identified Dirac-derived branches provides direct spectroscopic evidence for their participation in $c$-$f$ hybridization. Complementary measurements on independently cleaved samples and an effective hybridization model connect these branches to the Kondo heavy-quasiparticle spectrum. The combined results support preservation of nonsymmorphic Dirac-band connectivity under Kondo renormalization, showing how symmetry-constrained conduction states become constituents of correlated heavy quasiparticles.

### Paper [31]
Title: Multifractality in sputtered crystalline and amorphous transition metal nitride thin films
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10427
Authors: K. Anantha Padmanabhan, Kuraganti Vasu, P. Manimaran, M. Ghanashyam Krishna
Abstract: In this work, we present evidence for multifractal growth in Titanium and Niobium nitride thin films on different substrates. Self-similar multifractal structures such as dendrites and multi-branched snowflake-like structures are reported. The fractal dimensions are controlled by a combination of processing parameters and conditions at the film-substrate interface. Significantly, fractal growth is observed not only in crystalline films on crystalline and amorphous substrates but also on amorphous films on crystalline substrates. The 2D Multifractal Detrended Fluctuation Analysis (2D MFDFA) carried out on the scanning electron micrographs, clearly reveals the multifractal nature of the nitride thin films. The statistical moments were varied between -5 to +5. The Hausdorff dimensions for the TiN films are in the range of 1.65 to 1.79 while it is 1.64 to 1.74 for the NbN thin films. It is proposed that the observed multifractality is due to a competition between morphological instabilities and Diffusion-Limited Aggregation

### Paper [32]
Title: Transverse quasiparticle heat transport in p-wave magnetic Josephson junctions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10442
Authors: Jin-Xing Hou, Pritam Chatterjee, Song-Bo Zhang
Abstract: We study transverse heat currents carried by Bogoliubov quasiparticles in $p$-wave magnetic Josephson junctions under a longitudinal temperature bias. We find that such a junction supports a transverse spin heat current while the net transverse heat current vanishes. This effect stems from the $p$-wave magnetism, which shifts the two spin sectors oppositely in transverse momentum space and thereby causes them to carry opposite heat currents. A weak Zeeman field lifts the compensation between the two spin-sector contributions, thereby generating a finite net transverse heat current. The directions of both currents can be switched by reversing the sign of the $p$-wave magnetic splitting or tuning the superconducting phase difference, while both currents vanish in the absence of $p$-wave magnetism. These findings highlight $p$-wave magnetic Josephson junctions as a versatile platform for switchable transverse heat transport and controllable spin caloritronics.

### Paper [33]
Title: Extended No-Go Theorem and Its Verification in Spin-Selective Transport through Chiral Cyclodextrin Assemblies
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10449
Authors: Kai-Yuan Zhang, Shu-Zheng Zhou, Xi Sun, Hua-Hua Fu
Abstract: The classical two-terminal single-channel no-go theorem relies on time-reversal symmetry (TRS) and scattering-matrix unitarity. In open systems, omitted channels or environment leakage break unitarity, so TRS and the symplectic reciprocity symmetry TRS\(^{\dagger}\) are no longer equivalent, invalidating the classical premises. We prove that if the two-terminal single-channel scattering matrix satisfies TRS\(^{\dagger}\) plus central inversion symmetry, transmission remains no spin selectivity; this result provides an important complement to the classical no-go theorem in nonunitary regime. When these conditions are violated or the single-channel constraint is lifted, spin-selective transport occurs. In charge transport through devices based on chiral \(\gamma\)-cyclodextrin molecular assemblies, we verify separately the classical no-go theorem in unitary case and its supplementary version together with its multichannel corollary in nonunitary case: when the conditions for the corresponding no-go theorem hold, the chirality-induced spin selectivity (CISS) of the assembly disappears, whereas when they are violated, CISS emerges. These results extend the no-go theorem to nonunitary scattering systems and establish an effective theoretical framework for studying CISS in real molecular assemblies.

### Paper [34]
Title: Pseudogap in hole doped cuprates: a status report
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10473
Authors: Saleh Hasan Naqib, Raihana Shams Islam
Abstract: The pseudogap state, an anomalous momentum dependent partial suppression of low energy electronic excitations persisting well above the superconducting critical temperature in underdoped cuprates, remains one of the most profound enigmas in condensed physics today. This review summarizes over three decades of experimental and theoretical progress. It appears that pseudogap is not a mere precursor to superconductivity, but a distinct, intertwined state of matter characterized by a complex interplay of competing and cooperating electronic orders. We critically examine the bulk probes like resistivity and magnetic susceptibility and the surface hallmark spectroscopic signatures observed via angle resolved photoemission spectroscopy (ARPES) in this review. ARPES reveals a Fermi surface truncated into disconnected segments and scanning tunneling microscopy (STM) visualizes a spatially heterogeneous energy gap around the Fermi level. These observations are contextualized alongside transport anomalies, including resistivity and bulk magnetic susceptibility, to construct a phenomenological portrait of a phase that violates conventional Fermi liquid theory. By mapping the electronic phase diagram, we highlight the abrupt transition at above optimal doping. We conclude with open questions still to be answered. This review underscores the role of the pseudogap as a key to unlocking the unconventional pairing mechanism of hole doped cuprates and the broader physics of strongly correlated electron systems.

### Paper [35]
Title: Dopant Cluster Engineering for Tunable Room-Temperature Ferromagnetism in 2D Transition Metal Dichalcogenides
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10480
Authors: Shivangee, Sachin Grewal, Akash Singh
Abstract: Two-dimensional transition metal dichalcogenides (2D-TMDs) offer a promising platform for realizing dilute magnetic semiconductors. However, simultaneously achieving large magnetic anisotropy energy (MAE) and high Curie temperature (T$_\text{c}$) remains a major challenge. In this work, we employ DFT+U to systematically investigate the thermodynamic stability and magnetic properties of 2D-MoSe$_2$ and 2D-WSe$_2$ doped with transition metal atoms. We found that the paired dopant configurations are more stable than previously attributed isolated dopants. Despite their nearly identical crystal structures, 2D-MoSe$_2$ and 2D-WSe$_2$ show contrasting properties upon doping. All paired dopants in 2D-MoSe$_2$ exhibit long-range ferromagnetic coupling (FMC) primarily due to carrier-mediated exchange interactions, whereas those in 2D-WSe$_2$ show weak exchange coupling. Interestingly, 2Mn$_\text{2W}$ shows a transition from less stable antiferromagnetic coupling (AFMC) in the short range to highly stable FMC in the long-range. The combined effect of spin-orbit coupling, exchange interactions, and orbital hybridization gives rise to a large MAE with maximum in-plane values of $-$3.5 meV for 2V$_\text{2Mo}$ and $-$3.2 meV for 2Mn$_\text{2W}$. Monte Carlo simulations estimate T$_\text{c}$ $\simeq$ 440 K for 2Fe$_\text{2Mo}$ and ensure its high-temperature stability in 2D-MoSe$_2$. Our results establish dopant clustering as an effective route to engineer high-temperature ferromagnetism in 2D-TMDs and highlight their potential for nano-spintronic applications.

### Paper [36]
Title: Thouless pumping and Haldane-model topology in a driven extended SSH chain
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10507
Authors: Zhen Dong, Xin-Lei Zhao, Yi Zheng
Abstract: Thouless pumping provides a powerful route for probing Chern topology through quantized motion in one-dimensional lattices. We investigate such a topological pump in an extended Su-Schrieffer-Heeger (SSH) model with tunable next-nearest-neighbor hoppings. A graphical representation in Bloch space is used to construct pumping schemes. In this framework, the Bloch vector traces a closed loop that evolves in time. The Chern number of the synthetic band is then determined by the motion of this loop. Quantum pumping phenomena are demonstrated for a filled band and for a bosonic wave packet driven by Bloch oscillations. We further show that the Haldane model can be mapped to the driven extended SSH chain by replacing a quasimomentum by the pumping time. The quantized charge pump indicates that the Chern number of a two-dimensional model can be read out from a one-dimensional pumping protocol, offering an accessible route for detecting higher-dimensional topology, such as in photonic and cold-atom platforms.

### Paper [37]
Title: Quartet ferromagnetism in the small-exchange limit
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10512
Authors: Samuel Ogundeji, Tianxiong Han, Tyler J. Slade, Olivia Medina, Joanna M. Blawat, Filip Ronning, John Singleton, Sean M. Thomas, Alimamy F. Bangura, Wei Tian, Eleanor M. Clements, Bing Li, Adam A. Aczel, Paul C. Canfield, Benjamin G. Ueland, Rebecca Flint, Robert J. McQueeney, Aashish Sapkota
Abstract: Rare-earth magnets are often regarded as classical owing to their large angular momentum J, but this picture breaks down when exchange is smaller than the crystalline electric-field (CEF) energy scale, allowing the degeneracy of low-lying CEF manifolds to dictate the emergent quantum phases. Here, we uncover such a regime in the RV6Sn6 (R = rare-earth) kagome metals with strong magnetic anisotropy. Thermodynamic and neutron-diffraction measurements reveal successive axial and tilted ferromagnetic (FM) phases in DyV6Sn6, arising from independent ordering of the axial Mz and transverse Mx dipolar components enabled by a rare quasi-quartet ground state. On the other hand, HoV6Sn6 has a similar tilted magnetic anisotropy, but remains axial down to 0.03 K. This discrepancy can be traced back to the non-Kramers nature of Ho3+ where the CEF lifts the quartet degeneracy, leaving a quasi-triplet ground state. Unlike quartets or higher multiplets, we find that the effective spin-1 triplet does not allow independent dipolar ordering without strong quadrupolar (nematic) interactions that act to break rotational symmetry, revealing an essential multipolar prerequisite for tilted magnetic ground states. These results establish a quartet CEF manifold as the minimal degeneracy required for dipolar-driven independent ordering. Mapping the surrounding CEF parameter space further reveals competing magnetic, non-magnetic singlet, and potential quadrupolar phases, driven by multifold ground state degeneracies. These results establish CEF tuning as a versatile route to unconventional quantum phases across the broader RT6X6 (T = transition metal; X = Sn, Ge) family.

### Paper [38]
Title: Dipolar Exchange and Emergent Electric-Dipole-Carrying Quasiparticles in Electronic Ferroelectrics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10513
Authors: Ping Tang, Eiji Saitoh
Abstract: Electronic correlations underpin diverse spontaneously ordered states in matter, whose collective excitations emerge as quasiparticles that encode the underlying order. While spin exchange interactions give rise to magnetic order and its quasiparticle excitations known as magnons, their counterparts in electronic ferroelectrics remain poorly understood. Here, we derive an analogous quantum dipolar-pseudospin exchange model for electronic ferroelectricity and identify the associated bosonic quasiparticles that carry intrinsic electric dipole moments. Unlike ferrons in conventional lattice-based ferroelectrics, these dipolar quasiparticles arise entirely from collective electronic dynamics without involving lattice vibrations. Their excitation spectrum exhibits a linear Stark shift under a static electric field, while coupling to an oscillating electric field enables their coherent excitation, leading to colossal, resonantly enhanced second-harmonic generation and optical rectification in the terahertz regime. These dipolar quasiparticles offer promising opportunities for electrically tunable nonlinear optics and open a route toward extending the paradigm of transport in electronic systems beyond conventional charge currents to electric dipole currents.

### Paper [39]
Title: Pinching an elastic potato chip
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10546
Authors: Kosei Kataoka, Tomohiko G. Sano
Abstract: Thin shells deform by transmitting stress across their surface, yet how a shell of general shape carries a localized load remains unclear. Combining experiments, simulations, and theory on pinched saddle-shaped elastic shells, we show that the asymptotic lines, curves of zero normal curvature, dictate both deformation and stiffness. Isometry turns the shell mid-surface into a medium obeying a wave equation in space. Material points lit by the pinch are lifted, while those in its shadow sink. Finite thickness blurs the lit--shadow boundary, and the same rule maps where a clamped shell is rigid. Our findings indicate that light and shadow offer a unified framework for understanding mechanical stress propagation along shell structures.

### Paper [40]
Title: Markov-Shielded Typicality for Thermal Quantum States
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10593
Authors: Atsushi Iwaki, Chisa Hotta
Abstract: Pure states of quantum many-body systems at thermal equilibrium are known to be typical, in a sense that a randomly chosen pure state from an appropriate energy shell reproduces the Gibbs-state expectation values of most observables acting on a small subsystem. However, such typical states are intrinsically stochastic, conventionally generated from the random high-temperature state, suffer from statistical fluctuations, and do not provide a continuous connection to the ground state. Here, we introduce a deterministic variational framework for constructing a Markov-Shielded Typical (MST) state, a unique thermal pure-state representative whose local reduced density matrices closely approximate those of the Gibbs state, from the ground state up to infinite-temperature limit. Our approach reformulates free-energy minimization by replacing the nonlocal von Neumann entropy with a local, computationally tractable Markov entropy, where we establish rigorous upper and lower bounds of MST free energy to lie in the very vicinity of the thermal free energy. This framework extends the concept of typicality to deterministic pure-state representations of thermal equilibrium, offering a scalable wave-function approach with potential applications to both classical and quantum computing.

### Paper [41]
Title: Hyperuniformity in active matter
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10605
Authors: Hartmut L\"owen, Erdal C. O\u{g}uz
Abstract: Disordered hyperuniform systems are characterized by an anomalously strong suppression of long-wavelength density fluctuations. As a result, these unusual materials behave as though virtually incompressible, even though the particle arrangement itself can remain fully disordered at short range. This peculiar state of matter has been the topic of intense research in physics and mathematics in recent years. Another flourishing research arena is active matter, composed of self-propelled agents that convert energy into motion. While the two fields remained largely disjunct for a long time, the paper by Lei and coworkers (2019) created a link between hyperuniformity and active matter by demonstrating that interacting circle swimmers self-organize into disordered hyperuniform structures. Inspired by this work, many other examples of hyperuniformity in active matter have been identified. We shall describe the impact and outgrowth of this development, and give a brief perspective on further links between hyperuniformity and active matter.

### Paper [42]
Title: Dynamic Scaling and Aging in Hydrodynamic Coarsening of Vapor Bubbles in a Lennard-Jones Fluid
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10621
Authors: Sutapa Roy
Abstract: We investigate the coarsening and aging dynamics of vapor bubbles during liquid-vapor phase separation in a three-dimensional single-component Lennard-Jones fluid using large-scale molecular dynamics simulations. Following a temperature quench from a homogeneous high-temperature state into the two-phase coexistence region, vapor bubbles nucleate and grow in the liquid background. The characteristic bubble size follows a power-law growth, $\ell(t)\sim t^\alpha$, with a growth exponent $\alpha \simeq 2/3$, characteristic of inertial hydrodynamic coarsening. We also investigate the two-time autocorrelation function $C_{ag}(t,t_w)$ which follows waiting-time dependent relaxation, revealing aging. Furthermore, data at different waiting times collapse onto a scaling function when plotted as a function of the length ratio $\ell(t)/\ell(t_w)$, demonstrating dynamical scaling during aging. The corresponding scaling function exhibits an algebraic decay with an exponent $\lambda \simeq 3$. Interestingly, bubble and droplet growth exhibit distinctly different domain-growth and aging exponents despite both being disconnected morphologies.

### Paper [43]
Title: Ferromagnetic resonance signatures of edge states for distinguishing chiral and helical $p$-wave superconductors
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10623
Authors: Shun Muto, Yuya Ominato, Takeo Kato, Mamoru Matsuo, Ai Yamakage
Abstract: We theoretically investigate ferromagnetic resonance (FMR) modulation at interfaces between ferromagnetic insulators and two-dimensional chiral and helical $p$-wave superconductors. We evaluate the surface dynamical spin susceptibility, including both edge and bulk quasiparticle excitations. For momentum-conserving interfacial exchange at zero temperature, the edge contribution to the Gilbert damping exhibits distinct frequency scalings for the excitation energies well above the spectral broadening, $\hbar\omega\gg2\delta$: $\delta/(\hbar\omega)^{2}$ for the chiral state and $1/\hbar\omega$ for the helical state. For a clean superconducting boundary, edge-induced Ising spin anisotropy persists even when interfacial exchange disorder randomizes momentum transfer. The low-energy edge response is suppressed when the magnetization aligns with the edge-state spin axis. These results establish FMR modulation as a probe complementary to existing methods for distinguishing the chiral and helical states and constraining their $d$-vector directions.

### Paper [44]
Title: This Is Water: Relative Humidity as a Hidden Parameter in the Self-Assembly of CdSe Nanoplatelets into Threads
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10625
Authors: Benoit Wagnon, Seungho Lee, Lilian Guillemeney, Fr\'ed\'eric Chaput, Rodolphe Valleix, Laurent Coolen, Benjamin Ab\'ecassis
Abstract: When nanocrystals order into large-scale assemblies, interparticle coupling can give rise to new properties. In the case of semi-conducting nanoplatelets (NPL), stacking them orderly into threads yields highly efficient, ultrafast F\"orster Resonance Energy Transfer (FRET) over distances of hundreds of nanometers. Producing such assemblies reliably and reproducibly therefore matters. Yet, the mechanism by which dispersed NPL form stacked threads under evaporative self-assembly remains unclear. Here, we show that the relative humidity (RH) under which the NPL dispersion evaporation occurs is a critical, and so far overlooked, experimental parameter. Using a humidity-controlled chamber, we find that micrometer-long NPL stacks form only when evaporation occurs at RH > 50% rather than under dry conditions and when free oleic acid is present in the dispersion. Because such values lie within typical laboratory conditions, humidity is an easily overlooked parameter. We discuss how adventitious water may modify colloidal forces between NPL, through changes in solvent polarity, depletion, capillary or solvation interactions. Our experiments demonstrate that relative humidity is a key parameter for nanoparticle self-assembly and, more broadly, point to colloidal interactions at the nanoscale.

### Paper [45]
Title: Strong Collective Dynamics Hidden behind a Nearly Free Single-Particle Spectrum
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10636
Authors: Zhixuan Zhao, Sicheng He, Hanxiang Di, Nayun Chen, Jiabai Xiong, Guyixue Gao, Yanling Wu, Jun Li
Abstract: Single-particle spectroscopy has long served as a criterion for the strength of electronic correlations: strong collective motion is expected to shift and broaden spectral peaks and to renormalize the dispersion. Here we show that this correspondence can fail on the same microscopic orbital. The mechanism relies on local interference between two resonant side orbitals: one suppresses low-energy real transitions into the interacting environment, while the other suppresses spectral-weight backflow to the source orbital; virtual mixing nevertheless transfers the environmental spin polarization to the source. We prove a rigorous relative spectral bound that is uniform in environment size and interaction strength and holds for arbitrary stationary states; the protection becomes stronger as the relative frequency resolution improves. In a fully quantum two-cell device, a collective mode softens by 60%, whereas the particle intensity at the free peak changes by only 3.6 parts per million. Quantum Monte Carlo shows that the transferred spin response grows with spatial correlations and is quantitatively predicted by the free-band polarization. These results recast the meaning of a simple particle spectrum and provide a design principle for quantum probes that combine predictable particle spectra with strong collective sensitivity.

### Paper [46]
Title: Identifying Contributions to the Superconducting Diode Effect through Nonreciprocal Superconducting Properties
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10669
Authors: Ryo Iijima, Hisakazu Matsuki, Fugo Tokoro, Susumu Hirata, Akito Daido, Ryusuke Hisatomi, Yoichi Shiota, Shutaro Karube, Youichi Yanase, Teruo Ono
Abstract: Nonreciprocal superconducting responses provide a sensitive probe of superconducting states in systems where inversion and time-reversal symmetries are broken. However, in the superconducting diode effect (SDE), where the critical current differs for opposite current directions, nonreciprocity can also arise from vortex-induced contributions caused by minute magnetic field misalignment, complicating its interpretation. Here, we investigate a noncentrosymmetric $[Nb/V/Ta]_{40}$ superlattice and demonstrate nonreciprocity in all three fundamental superconducting properties: the critical current, critical field, and superconducting transition temperature. By measuring the angular dependence of the nonreciprocal critical field $\Delta B_{\mathrm{c} 2}$, we separate the contribution of misalignment-induced out-of-plane vortices from the in-plane response. We identify a sharply enhanced SDE emerging near $B_{\mathrm{c} 2}$, accompanied by nonreciprocal responses in $T_{\mathrm{c}}$. These observations exhibit phenomenology similar to that reported in layered superconductors, where an orbital-FFLO state with finite-momentum Cooper pairing has been reported. Our results establish a protocol for assessing out-of-plane vortex contributions to the superconducting diode effect and demonstrate that nonreciprocal responses provide complementary probes of unconventional superconducting states.

### Paper [47]
Title: Sub-Thouless energy scale in interface-dominated unconventional superconductor junctions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10675
Authors: L\'eon Goedegebuur, Tim Kokkeler, Yukio Tanaka
Abstract: We derive the conductance in unconventional superconductor - normal metal - normal metal junctions, taking into account the resistance between the normal metals. We show that applying Tanaka--Nazarov boundary conditions at both superconductor and normal metal interfaces reveals a new characteristic low-energy scale which is sub-Thouless in the experimentally relevant strong-interface regime. This scale governs, among other quantities, the curvature of the local density of states (LDOS) and the width of conductance dips near the zero-bias conductance peak in triplet superconductor junctions, thereby replacing the Thouless energy as the dominant low-energy parameter. We derive universal $s$-wave proximitized diffusive metal expressions for the zero-bias LDOS and differential conductance which solely depend on the interface and bulk resistances, independent of the superconducting gap and Thouless energy scale. This framework provides a possible origin of the anomalous low-bias conductance features observed in unconventional superconductors, offering a refined mechanism for interpreting interface and correlated electron physics in experimental hybrid junctions.

### Paper [48]
Title: Scalable MoS2/Si Photodiode Arrays from Roll-to-Roll Mechanical Exfoliation
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10696
Authors: Yigit Sozen, Thomas Pucher, Andres Castellanos-Gomez
Abstract: We present a scalable, all-dry route to MoS2/Si photodiodes based on high-throughput roll-to-roll mechanical exfoliation. The method yields dense films of large-lateral-size MoS2 nanosheets and enables the formation of heterojunctions that operate in the photovoltaic regime when transferred onto locally etched Si regions. Individual devices show robust, fast photoresponses and broadband wavelength sensitivity. We further implemented a linear pixel array demonstrating position sensing capability and a 4x4 array as a proof-of-concept imaging sensor. These results indicate that roll-to-roll mechanical exfoliation can overcome common limitations of solution-processed films to enable low-cost, self-powered optoelectronic detectors for sensing and imaging applications.

### Paper [49]
Title: Probing Chern Topology through Hall-Sensitive Photonic Spin Hall Shifts in a d-wave Altermagnet
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10706
Authors: Mahnoor Sajid, Maha Siddiqui, Muzamil Shah
Abstract: In this paper, we investigate the photonic spin Hall effect in a two-dimensional Rashba-coupled $d$-wave altermagnetic Chern system subjected to a uniform out-of-plane exchange field. We first analyze the energy-band structure and the Dirac mass distribution at the high-symmetry points, where $m_{\Gamma}=m_M=\Delta_x, m_X=\Delta_x-4 t_J$, and $m_Y=\Delta_x+4 t_J$. Band inversions at high-symmetry points generate both a topologically trivial regime $(C=0)$ and Chern-insulating phases $(C= \pm 1)$. Employing the Kubo formalism for the optical conductivity together with the reflection matrix for a substrate-supported conducting sheet, we compute helicity-dependent finite-waist displacements of a reflected Gaussian beam. The resulting optical response exhibits a minimum interband absorption threshold, a Van Hove-type spectral feature, and distinct transitions at the $X$ and $Y$ points arising from the momentum dependence of the exchange field. Under the specified Hall-tensor and polarization sign conventions, the longitudinal helicity half-separation is odd upon reversal of the optical Hall conductivity and correspondingly changes sign between opposite-Chern phases, whereas the transverse half-separation is even in the Hall response. The maximal spin-dependent splitting occur in the vicinity of the pseudo-Brewster reflectance minimum. The longitudinal spin splitting reverses sign between opposite Chern phases, reflecting the exchange-controlled optical Hall response, while its pronounced spectral variations reveal the influence of inequivalent interband transitions induced by the $d$-wave altermagnetic exchange. These findings establish polarization-resolved photonic beam shifts as a sensitive probe of Hall-active topological phases and their characteristic optical spectra.

### Paper [50]
Title: A new approach to measuring both the steady flow curve and the planar extensional viscosity of complex fluids using exponential shear
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10780
Authors: Nicholas King, Eugene Pashkovski, Reid Patterson, Gareth H. McKinley
Abstract: We revisit the protocol known as Exponential Shear (ES), first proposed by Doshi and Dealy in 1986. This is a transient strong flow that can be readily imposed in the laboratory using commercial strain-controlled (separate motor and transducer) rheometers which accurately measure both the (exponentially) time-varying torque and normal force exerted by the sample. Numerous studies over the years have proposed competing definitions of an appropriate material function for reporting data obtained from the exponentially time-varying imposed deformation. We revisit this unresolved challenge and carefully reconsider the exponential material kinematics involved and the proper definition of the corresponding material functions. We show that by understanding the time-varying eigenvalues and eigenvectors of both the shearing kinematics and the evolving state of stress in the sample it is, in fact, possible to use an exponential shear test to quantitatively map out \textit{both} the full flow curve (of shear viscosity vs. shear rate) and the steady planar extensional viscosity curve as a function of deformation rate. These findings are supported by numerical simulations of a number of nonlinear viscoelastic constitutive models, asymptotic analysis, as well as experiments with several polymeric liquids.

### Paper [51]
Title: Ising model on Calandre lattice
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10834
Authors: F. A. Kassan-Ogly, A. V. Zarubin
Abstract: In this paper, we proposed to consideration a noval (not yet exploited) planar lattice --- Calandre lattice with the Ising model applied to it.
