Finite-element simulations show that compressively stressed TiN films can generate shear strains exceeding 0.1\% at the quantum well, sufficient for deterministic valley splittings of hundreds of $\mu \text{eV}$ in strain-assisted Wiggle Wells. We show that this strain can extend across multiple quantum dots and is maintained in realistic gate geometries, providing a fabrication-compatible approach for scaling deterministic valley splitting to quantum dot arrays.

### Paper [156]
Title: Tetragonal-to-orthorhombic distortion in CeRh$_{2}$As$_{2}$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12873
Authors: P. Khanenko (Hochfeld-Magnetlabor Dresden), M. Pfeiffer (Max Planck Institute for Chemical Physics of Solids, Dresden, Germany), K. Semeniuk (Max Planck Institute for Chemical Physics of Solids, Dresden, Germany), E. Hassinger (Max Planck Institute for Chemical Physics of Solids, Dresden, Germany), P. Thalmeier (Max Planck Institute for Chemical Physics of Solids, Dresden, Germany), J. Wosnitza (Hochfeld-Magnetlabor Dresden), A. M. Le\'on (Departamento de F\'isica, Facultad de Ciencias, Universidad de Chile, Santiago, Chile), G. Zwicknagl (Institute for Mathematical Physics, Technische Universit\"at Braunschweig, Braunschweig, Germany), C. Geibel (Max Planck Institute for Chemical Physics of Solids, Dresden, Germany), S. Khim (Max Planck Institute for Chemical Physics of Solids, Dresden, Germany), M. Brando (Max Planck Institute for Chemical Physics of Solids, Dresden, Germany)
Abstract: CeRh$_{2}$As$_{2}$ is best known for its two superconducting phases, which are observed when a magnetic field is applied along the $c$ axis of its tetragonal unit cell. The emergence of superconductivity, with $T_{c} \approx 0.31$K, is preceded by an ordered state (phase I), with an ordering temperature $T_{0} \approx 0.46$K. The precise nature of the order parameter in phase I is still unknown, although evidence of dipolar ordering was found. To investigate a possible symmetry breaking at $T_{\textrm{0}}$, we conducted longitudinal thermal-expansion measurements along the crystallographic [100], [010], [110], and [1$\overline{1}$0] directions on a single-crystalline sample cut into an octagonal shape. Our results demonstrate the breaking of the fourfold in-plane symmetry below $T_{\textrm{0}}$, evidencing a small tetragonal-to-orthorhombic distortion (orthorhombicity $\delta \sim 10^{-7}$). The breaking of the tetragonal symmetry at $T_{\textrm{0}}$ might imply that the superconducting order parameter that develops within phase I cannot be multi-component. More generally, the distortion places restrictions on the possible order parameters for phase I.

### Paper [157]
Title: Magnetic behavior of the $5d^1$ Re-based double perovskite Sr$_2$ZnReO$_6$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04071
Authors: Muhammad Maikudi Isah, Biswajit Dalal, Xun Kang, Dario Fiore Mosca, Ifeanyi John Onuorah, Valerio Scagnoli, Pietro Bonf\`a, Roberto De Renzi, Alexei A. Belik, Cesare Franchini, Kazunari Yamaura, Samuele Sanna
Abstract: The subtle interplay between spin-orbit coupling, exchange interactions, and cation ordering can lead to exotic magnetic states in transition-metal ions. We report a comprehensive study of the Re-based (5$d^1$) ordered double perovskite oxide Sr$_2$ZnReO$_6$ combining synchrotron x-ray diffraction (XRD), magnetic susceptibility, muon spin relaxation ($\mu$SR) measurements, and density functional theory (DFT) calculations. XRD reveals that Sr$_2$ZnReO$_6$ crystallizes in the monoclinic structure (space group $P2_1/n$) at low temperature. Magnetic susceptibility data indicate a transition below $\sim$13 K, with $M$--$H$ loops showing ferromagnetic-like hysteresis and an unusually high coercive field of 23 kOe at 2 K. Zero-field $\mu$SR measurements detect static and spatially disordered internal fields below $T_M \simeq $ 12 K, consistent with a canted antiferromagnetic ground state determined by detailed DFT and force-theorem in Hubbard-I calculations. The reduced high-temperature effective moment ($\sim0.76~\mu_B$) and very small static moment ($\lesssim 0.2~\mu_B$) derived from $\mu$SR analysis and local-field simulations indicate a decisive role of spin-orbit coupling. Through a combined experimental and computational approach we unambiguously determine the canted antiferromagnetic order in Sr$_2$ZnReO$_6$, showing that a very small ordered moment coexists with an exceptionally large coercivity. These results underscore the crucial role of spin-orbit coupling and orbital ordering, providing new insights into magnetism in 5$d^1$ double perovskites.

### Paper [158]
Title: Twisted Compactification, Quotient Quantum Codes and Logical Gates
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09538
Authors: Po-Shen Hsin
Abstract: Many quantum codes, including balanced-product, bivariate-bicycle and genon codes and the five-qubit code, are quotient codes: a larger code folded onto itself by identifying each check with its image under a deck transformation composed with a symmetry. We show that this is the lattice form of twisted compactification, in which a theory on $X\times F$ carries a symmetry background along $F$, and we use the correspondence in both directions. For monomial quotients of Pauli stabilizer codes, a transfer theorem gives the logical qudits, logical operators and distance bounds from those of the parent, with a Tate cohomology group counting the logical operators that are new. For higher-form and cup-product symmetries the twist is an entangling diagonal circuit; when a finite cover trivializes the background, the code is a dressed quotient, so abelian Pauli codes give non-abelian topological order such as $D(D_4)$. The dressings have Clifford level at most one less than the degree of the twist, and for cubic twists the code dimension is a count of solutions of quadratic equations in cup products, which certifies that the code is not a Pauli stabilizer code when it is not a power of two; on tori and slabs the distance is linear in the size. Twists also create logical gates: three toric codes on $S^3$ have no logical qubits, but on $\mathbb{RP}^3$ the same circuit is a logical $CCZ$. A quarter-turn twist of the 3D fermionic toric code gives codes of linear distance, from $[[768,2,9]]$ up, whose $p+ip$ pump is an explicit finite-time quasi-local evolution that preserves the code space exactly; using the spin-topological response formula of Barkeshli, Hsin and Kobayashi, we identify its logical action as $T$ up to Clifford factors. Finally, twisting abelian gauge theories reach only the quantum doubles $D(G)$ with $[G,G]$ central.

### Paper [159]
Title: Prestatability in the Macroscopic Limit
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09989
Authors: Andrea Palessandro
Abstract: We examine whether the variables required for a future macroscopic description of a given physical system can themselves be stated in advance. Representing macroscopic descriptions as coarse-grainings of a fixed microscopic phase space, we distinguish formal prestatability, concerning the existence of an effective complete vocabulary of admissible macroscopic distinctions, from dynamical prestatability, concerning whether the future relevance of those distinctions is decidable. Both obstructions disappear for finite microscopic state spaces. In the infinite limit, exhaustive formal prestatability can fail, while a restricted predictive architecture may remain formally pre-statable. We construct such an architecture and show that, under universal dynamics, the future relevance of a candidate variable is equivalent to the halting problem. Thus a fixed microscopic phase space can support macroscopic descriptions whose future variables are not fully pre-statable.

### Paper [160]
Title: Experimentally Grounded Machine Learning of PrecursorSurface-Reactant Chemistry in Atomic Layer Deposition
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10024
Authors: Bonwook Gu, Trinh Ngoc Le, Zunair Masroor, Minhyeok Lee, Han-Bo-Ram Lee
Abstract: Whether a precursor-substrate-reactant combination yields an atomic layer deposition (ALD) process is still settled by trial-and-error experiments. Here we introduce AJIN (ALD Joint Interpretable Network), a machine-learning framework that learns this decision from experimental outcomes. We compiled 560 experimentally observed ALD and area-selective ALD outcomes spanning 76 precursors, 27 substrates, 20 reactants and 25-620 degrees Celsius. Following the ALD cycle, AJIN predicts adsorption from graph-neural-network embeddings of the precursor and substrate with temperature, and deposition by adding the reactant. The best model (CHGNet-MLP) reaches balanced accuracies of 79.2% for adsorption and 84.8% for deposition and transfers partially to unseen precursors. SHAP and ablation analyses show that substrate chemistry governs adsorption, whereas precursor, substrate and reactant all contribute to deposition. Tested against our laboratory's ALD experiments, AJIN correctly predicted 83% of new-substrate cases and 71% of new precursor-substrate adsorption cases, providing an experimentally grounded tool for prioritizing ALD and area-selective ALD chemistries.

### Paper [161]
Title: Efficient classical simulation of Fermi-Hubbard models with Kramers symmetry
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10039
Authors: Chaithanya Rayudu, Jun Takahashi, Ojas Parekh, Andrew Zhao
Abstract: Quantum Monte Carlo methods are widely used to solve quantum many-body problems. However, rigorous guarantees for their performance are poorly understood, especially for simulating interacting fermions. In this work, we develop a quantum Monte Carlo algorithm for a broad family of fermionic systems exhibiting Kramers time-reversal symmetry, and prove that their partition functions can be estimated in polynomial time. This family, which contains many important classes of Hubbard-type models, is known to both avoid the sign problem and obey a Lee-Yang zero-freeness theorem. Thus our results suggest that these two properties together may enable efficient classical algorithms for quantum many-body systems more broadly.

### Paper [162]
Title: Small Defects at Large Charge
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10113
Authors: Jesse Woods
Abstract: We compute conformal data of cusped line defects with impurities for the abelian Higgs model in the semiclassical large-charge limit. By working perturbatively in the impurity charge $q$, we obtain the scaling dimension of the impurity to $\mathcal{O}(q^3)$ to all orders in the 't Hooft quartic and gauge couplings. Each term represents a resummation of infinitely many Feynman diagrams. Near the critical gauge coupling where conformality is lost, we resum the leading singular behaviour to all orders in $q$. We also comment on the spectrum of excited cusp states at $\mathcal{O}(q)$. Additionally, we compute the leading contributions to the Bremsstrahlung function, the coefficient of the leading irrelevant operator in the fusion limit, and argue that the Casimir energy receives no corrections semiclassically. Our results agree with known perturbative data and numerical solutions. We compare them with recent bounds on cusps. We comment on the interplay between the large rapidity limit and semiclassics.

### Paper [163]
Title: Entanglement percolation enhanced by the power of choice
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10119
Authors: Yanxuan Shao, Adilson E. Motter
Abstract: Quantum communication networks are best supported by maximally entangled states, but imperfections in entanglement generation and distribution generally limit the distributed states to being only partially entangled. Therefore, protocols typically consisting of local operations and classical communication are required to create suitably entangled networks. Here, we introduce a power-of-choice (PoC) protocol based on reversing the selection criteria in the Achlioptas process previously considered in classical explosive percolation. The resulting PoC protocol anticipates the entanglement percolation transition, thereby reducing the entanglement resources needed to establish quantum communication. Notably, in contrast to explosive percolation, the PoC protocol admits an implementation using local information with no appreciable loss of performance.

### Paper [164]
Title: Learning the interactions underlying multicellular migration using many-body stochastic inference
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10138
Authors: Bram J. Hoogland, Tom Brandst\"atter, Emily Brieger, Joachim O. R\"adler, Chase P. Broedersz
Abstract: Uncovering the interactions that give rise to emergent collective behavior in active and living matter remains a major challenge. Multicellular migration exemplifies this challenge: complex cell-cell interactions shape collective motion, but are difficult to measure directly. Here, we introduce a many-body stochastic inference framework that learns these interactions directly from experimental trajectories. We infer interaction models spanning different dynamical dependencies and complexities from short-time cell dynamics, and select among them based on their ability to predict emergent collective behaviors. Applied across three behaviorally distinct cell types, this approach yields minimal, predictive models of cell-cell interactions underlying multicellular migration. We find that non-cancerous epithelial cells coordinate multicellular migration through reciprocal repulsion and velocity alignment, while cancerous mesenchymal cells additionally exhibit previously unrecognized nonreciprocal interactions that couple to self-propulsion through two distinct mechanisms, which we term velocity inhibition in fibrosarcoma cells and velocity enhancement in breast cancer cells. Conceptually, these nonreciprocal interactions allow cells to tune the onset and speed of flocks, underscoring their role in collective migration and potentially enhancing cancer cell invasiveness at large cell densities. More broadly, our framework offers a general and principled route for extracting interaction laws of active and living systems directly from data.

### Paper [165]
Title: The satisfiability conjecture for random k-SAT formulas. Human-friendly exposition of the recent OpenAI resolution
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10139
Authors: David Gamarnik
Abstract: The satisfiability conjecture was one of the most important open problems in discrete probability. It was resolved recently first by Carenini~\cite{GCarenini}, and shortly after by OpenAI~\cite{k-sat-OpenAI}, among other 370 problems solved by its internal model. The conjecture concerned the satisfiability property of random instances of the k-SAT problem. It posits the existence of a fixed value for the clause-to-variables ratio around which the probability that the formula is satisfiable drops sharply from nearly one to nearly zero as the ratio increases. While the case of large constant $k$ was resolved a decade ago by Ding, Sly and Sun~\cite{ding2015proof,ding2022proof} using the heavy machinery of the second moment method, the case of general $k$ eluded proof until ~\cite{GCarenini} and~\cite{k-sat-OpenAI}.
  In this short note we provide a ''human-friendly'' and detailed exposition of the OpenAI's resolution of the conjecture.

### Paper [166]
Title: Regev's Reduction Reaches Reconstruction
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10283
Authors: Stephen P. Jordan, Quynh T. Nguyen, Leo Zhou
Abstract: Regev's reduction uses a quantum Fourier transform to carry out reductions between various problems related to lattices and codes. We apply it to the problem of sampling from Gibbs distributions of certain classical spin glass Hamiltonians, which can equivalently be thought of as cost functions for average-case instances of an optimization problem called $D$-regular max-$k$-LINSAT. This problem becomes hard at low temperature. Here, we use a quantum decoding algorithm called Belief Propagation with Quantum Messages (BPQM) to solve the decoding problem arising from Regev's reduction. Our main contribution is a proof that the minimum temperature reachable using Regev+BPQM is exactly equal to the reconstruction threshold temperature for noisy channels transmitting signals on a tree network. This equality can be understood via Renes' duality between classical and quantum channels, which we use to show that quantum decoding is possible exactly when classical reconstruction on the dual problem is impossible. M{\'e}zard and Montanari identified this reconstruction temperature with a dynamical glass transition predicted by the one-step replica-symmetry breaking (1RSB) cavity equation. It is conjectured that local samplers such as Metropolis Monte Carlo and Glauber dynamics cannot equilibrate efficiently below this reconstruction temperature but may do so above it; our large numerical experiments show close agreement with this prediction across hundreds of different problem parameters. Therefore, our results rigorously show that Regev+BPQM reaches a temperature for which, to our knowledge, there is no matching classical guarantee, and brings Regev+BPQM to the conjectured limit of efficient Gibbs sampling by classical local dynamics.

### Paper [167]
Title: On the universal structure in deterministic generative diffusion
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10338
Authors: Defang Chen, Zhenyu Zhou, Can Wang, Zeyu Zheng, Siwei Lyu
Abstract: Diffusion-based generative modeling has emerged as a general computational framework for simulating high-dimensional, structured data across various domains. Understanding its generative mechanisms is paramount for advancing model training and inference in a principled way. Here, we demonstrate that deterministic sampling trajectories share a universal geometric structure invariant to data modalities and diffusion model implementations. This structure can be largely explained by an exactly solvable model based on the optimal linear denoiser.

### Paper [168]
Title: Tuning fluid frictional drag through flow-induced aggregation of wormlike micelles
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10365
Authors: Lucas Warwaruk, Amanda Tiano, Berith Isaac, Gareth H. McKinley
Abstract: Charged amphiphilic surfactants are versatile polymorphic molecules which can form supramolecular structures, such as wormlike micelles. We study the rheology and non-Newtonian fluid dynamics of the cationic surfactant trimethyltetradecylammonium chloride (TTAC) combined with the aromatic counter-ion sodium salicylate (NaSal). In the non-dilute regime, this solution exhibits a linear viscoelastic response consistent with a single-mode Jeffreys model, and in capillary pinch-off experiments, fluid filaments form extended cylindrical threads, indicating strong strain-stiffening. In the dilute regime the linear viscoelasticity and extensional rheology become immeasurably small, but the TTAC/NaSal mixture develops flow-induced structures at high shear rates, as the result of an end-to-end concatenation of micelles produced by shear-induced flow alignment. These flow-induced structures augment both the measured viscosity and the viscous drag in laminar flows at high shear rates (by as much as 50 %). In turbulent Taylor-Couette flow, the flow-induced structures produce a sudden and dramatic destabilizing influence on the stable toroidal Taylor vortices, resulting in upwards of a 200 % enhancement in turbulent drag. However, with increasing Reynolds number, the turbulent flow transitions smoothly to a state of drag reduction, generating up to a 30 % reduction in fluid frictional drag but without the shear-induced degradation that plagues drag reduction strategies based on solutions of high molecular weight polymer, such as polyacrylamide. These results show that surfactants are a unique and effective modifier of fluid frictional drag in both laminar and turbulent flow regimes, capable of producing both significant levels of drag enhancement and drag reduction by tuning the composition and operating conditions.

### Paper [169]
Title: Quantitative Assessment of Crystal Structure Prediction
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10380
Authors: Sergio Rinc\'on, Gabriel Gonz\'alez, Nicol\'as Andrade, Rafael Vel\'asquez, Sebasti\'an Ojeda, Juanita Puentes, Nicol\'as Aparicio, Paula C\'ardenas, Pablo Arbel\'aez
Abstract: Crystal Structure Prediction (CSP) aims to predict a material's 3D structure from its chemical composition, with applications from energy storage to biomedical devices. Despite its importance, existing evaluation frameworks suffer from key limitations: they rely on threshold-sensitive or incomparable metrics, focus predominantly on geometric performance while overlooking physical plausibility, and fail to account for polymorphism, where a chemical composition can crystallize into multiple stable structures. In parallel, recent Symmetry-Informed CSP (SICSP) methods often report results alongside standard CSP baselines while receiving crystallographic templates at inference, which constrain the search space and make SICSP a fundamentally different task. To address these limitations, we introduce an Orientation-invariant, Polymorph-Aware, Large Benchmark (OPAL-Bench) that evaluates CSP and SICSP as separate tasks. We evaluate 7 CSP and 2 SICSP methods under OPAL-Bench across 4 datasets using threshold-free geometric and physical distance metrics. Our results show that no method dominates across all metrics, and that geometric performance is not strongly correlated with physical plausibility. We also release OPAL-CSP, an SE(3)-equivariant flow-matching baseline for the CSP track that performs competitively on geometric metrics and ranks among the best on physical ones. By unifying geometric and physical metrics under a polymorph-aware protocol, OPAL-Bench enables a more comprehensive assessment of generative quality in CSP.

### Paper [170]
Title: Dynamic Molecular Transformation Mediated Crystallization Regulation and Interface Passivation in Tin Based Perovskite Solar Cells
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10420
Authors: Ying Tang, Zuhong Zhang, Hongzhuo Wu, Zhihao Li, Zhenhuang Su, Yonggang Yang, Hairui Liu, Feng Yang, Zhe Li, Luca Sapienza, Jorge Pascual, Shengnan Zuo, Bo Hou, Antonio Abate, Meng Li, Yufang Liu
Abstract: Tin-based perovskites are promising candidates for lead-free photovoltaics but suffer from labile Sn$^{2+}$ coordination, rapid crystallization, and severe interfacial defects. Here, we develop a dynamic molecular transformation using 1-acetylpiperazine (PX) to regulate crystallization and interface chemistry simultaneously. During nucleation and growth, the carbonyl group coordinates with Sn$^{2+}$ to stabilize precursor complexes, moderate supersaturation, and enable controlled crystal growth. Upon annealing, PX undergoes in situ molecular transformation, suggested to form amidinium-functionalized species that preferentially occupy surface A-site vacancies, forming a passivation layer and suppressing nonradiative recombination. This coupled bulk-to-interface evolution yields compact, highly crystalline films with improved optoelectronic quality. Devices achieve a power conversion efficiency of 15.18% and retain 94.1% of initial efficiency after 500 h of continuous illumination. This work demonstrates a dynamic molecular engineering approach integrating crystallization control and interface passivation, providing a pathway toward efficient and stable lead-free perovskite photovoltaics.

### Paper [171]
Title: Is price-impact decay statistically identifiable by propagator estimation? Diffusive prices force the inferred kernel to be transient and fine-tuned
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10574
Authors: Shunta Fujiwara, Yuki Sato, Kiyoshi Kanazawa
Abstract: Despite the long memory of market-order flow, price dynamics remain diffusive---a seemingly paradoxical coexistence known in econophysics as the diffusivity puzzle. The propagator model has long been regarded as a promising solution: price impact decays in time, finely balanced against the order-flow memory, and propagators inferred from data have been reported to be consistent with this fine balance. Here we prove a no-go theorem: asymptotic impact decay is not statistically identifiable by propagator estimation, since for diffusive prices the fine-tuned decay is returned whether or not the true impact decays. It rests on universal theorems for the Wiener--Hopf deconvolution by which propagators are inferred under long-memory order flow: (i) the inferred propagator is necessarily transient, even when the true impact is permanent; (ii) under a mild condition, it decays with the fine-balance exponent, fixed by the order-flow autocorrelation alone. The fine-tuned decay is thus a property of the deconvolution, not evidence of impact decay. We demonstrate this with an exactly solvable null model that retains order splitting and the square-root law but whose impact is strictly permanent: the inferred propagator shows an artificial fine-tuned decay although the true impact never decays. Nonlinear propagator estimation, being of Wiener--Hopf type, is subject to the same theorems whenever true metaorders have long memory.

### Paper [172]
Title: DFT-Agent Bench: Scaffold Effects and Execution Provenance in Autonomous DFT
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10629
Authors: Shijie Liu, Jin Yang, Weilin Wan, Jingtao Han, Jia LI, Tianqi Deng
Abstract: Large language models are increasingly deployed as autonomous agents for first-principles materials simulation. We report empirical findings from DFT-Agent Bench, a benchmark of 200+ Quantum ESPRESSO tasks spanning relaxation, electronic structure, equation-of-state, and elastic-tensor pipelines over multiple material tiers. Each task carries a QE-derived ground truth with public-reference cross-checks where available, and a provenance audit checks whether the required QE execution contract was met. Under one execution harness, we compare a near-bare ReAct baseline with a bundled v5.1 scaffold on two models. The complete scaffold improves bands+DOS by +0.08/+0.11 and d-electron vc-relax by +0.17 on both models, while high-scoring relaxation and equation-of-state cells leave little room for improvement. The elastic result is model-dependent (0.18 to 0.40 for deepseek-v4-pro and 0.62 to 0.59 for claude-opus-4-8). A fixed 100-task analysis with retrieval disabled supports the aggregate pattern: the fully frozen DeepSeek comparison gains +0.17, while the Opus comparison gains +0.10 against a historical pool baseline (p < 0.001). The provenance gate catches incomplete or unmarked executions that can receive high rubric scores; it is an execution-contract check, not a test for intentional fraud. These results characterize the evaluated DFT workflows. The anonymous review artifact contains the frozen diagnostic slice, prompts, scorer, and solver revisions: https://anonymous.4open.science/r/231e31re91287h/README.md.

### Paper [173]
Title: Hidden Coherence and Controlled Quasihole Motion in a Fermionic Laughlin State on a Superconducting Quantum Processor
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10687
Authors: Kui Zhao, Hao Wu, Lei-Yi-Nan Liu, Gui-Han Liang, Cheng-Lin Deng, Cai-Ping Fang, Hao-Tian Liu, Wei-Ping Yuan, Qingyuan Wu, Ziting Wang, Wei-Guo Ma, Hao Li, Jia-Chi Zhang, Tian-Ming Li, Jia-Cheng Song, Zhen-Ting Bao, Zheng-He Liu, Yang He, Yong-Xi Xiao, Yu Liu, Zhongcheng Xiang, Dongning Zheng, Guangming Xue, Haifeng Yu, Kai Xu, Zhao Liu, Kaixuan Huang, Jian Cui, Heng Fan
Abstract: Fractional quantum Hall (FQH) states support fractionalized excitations and geometric phases, yet experimentally resolving the many-body coherence encoded in their wave functions remains challenging. Here, we prepare a three-electron $\nu=1/3$ Laughlin state on the Haldane sphere using seven superconducting qubits and reconstruct its many-body density matrix. Through Read's composite-boson construction, we reveal vortex-dressed coherence that remains appreciable over a larger separation range than electron coherence. The extended profile is suppressed in a Fock-diagonal reference constructed from the measured populations, demonstrating its dependence on off-diagonal many-body coherence. We further prepare a one-flux-added quasihole and characterize its fractional charge response against finite-size benchmarks. Controlled quasihole motion yields an effective geometric-phase signature from the reconstructed dominant-eigenstate trajectory. These complementary measurements provide microscopic access to the coherent structure and quasihole response of a finite fermionic Laughlin state, demonstrating an experimental route to probing strongly correlated FQH wave functions on programmable quantum processors.

### Paper [174]
Title: 2D polaritons for Squeezing and Shrinking Light
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10754
Authors: Eduardo Bede Barros, Niclas Sven M\"uller, Stephanie Reich, Christian Thomsen
Abstract: Two-dimensional (2D) materials have emerged as an extraordinary platform for polaritonics, given their remarkable ability to confine light to deep sub-wavelength scales. However, as material thicknesses reach the nanometer limit, macroscopic electrodynamic models become inadequate for the description of light-matter coupling in 2D materials, leaving a significant gap in our understanding of 2D polaritons. In this work, we develop a comprehensive quantum microscopic framework that describes 2D polaritons through a general, scale-less Hamiltonian. Starting from the quantum properties of a polarizable 2D surface, we derive collective excitations and we identify both transverse electric magnetic modes existing outside the light cone. Our results demonstrate that these 2D systems facilitate a powerful synergy between extreme wavelength shrinking and intrinsic quantum squeezing of electric field quadratures. By establishing this theoretical roadmap, our work provides the essential architecture for controlling non-classical states of light at the nanoscale, opening new avenues for high-precision quantum sensing and the development of next-generation optoelectronic devices

### Paper [175]
Title: Monodromy Defects and Bulk Renormalization Group Flows on the Fuzzy Sphere
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10815
Authors: Kimyeong Lee, Joydeep Naskar
Abstract: A monodromy around a defect specifies a symmetry transformation, but the low energy properties of the defect depend on its interaction with bulk degrees of freedom and also itself. We study this dependence on the fuzzy sphere. Along an anisotropy flow, an $O(3)$ half-twist approaches the $O(2)$ half-twist when its twisted components stay light and disappears when they become massive. At large $N$, untwisted spectators give a positive correction to the defect entropy, fixed by the conformal weight of the free defect. The surviving $O(2)$ monodromy admits a relevant charged perturbation that pins the line. Pinning before or after the easy-plane bulk flow gives matching infrared junction data. A pin of the untwisted component survives only the opposite, easy-axis flow. Walls between opposite pinnings test the stability of the candidate ordered line. They are relevant on the pinned $O(2)$ monodromy lines studied and favoured to be relevant, with a smaller margin, on the ordinary $O(2)$ line, while the ordinary $O(3)$ and $O(4)$ walls lie close to marginality. We separate the wall dimension into a tilt stiffness and an angular shape, comparing them with a Ward identity, a two-loop expansion and a large-$N$ saddle. In the fully twisted large-$N$ model, winding weakens the condensate and lowers the wall dimension and the entropy released by pinning. The resulting entropy comparison permits disordering into the unpinned line wherever the leading wall estimate is relevant. Finally, we construct the regular half-twist on the fuzzy four-sphere as a starting point for analogous questions in five dimensions.

### Paper [176]
Title: One Measurement, Many Fronts in Kitaev and Cluster Chains
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10912
Authors: Nicholas Musat, Stephan Haas, Rishabh Jha
Abstract: A projective measurement of one site in the ground state of a quantum chain disturbs the chain in two ways: learning the outcome shifts distant expectation values at once, through ground-state correlations, and the measurement erases the correlations between the measured site and the rest. Both changes travel outward. We solve this problem exactly for the Kitaev chain, the cluster chain in a transverse field, and the Kitaev chain with power-law couplings, and we find that far from the measured site every quadratic observable responds through a front. Where the front is and how fast it broadens depend only on the quasiparticle dispersion. How strong it is depends on two operators: how strongly the measured one creates the fastest quasiparticles and how strongly the observed one detects them; if either factor vanishes, the front is weaker and decays faster. One measurement therefore launches many fronts, one per observable, with a common position and width but different decay laws. In the Kitaev chain the density and the bond energy, the two terms of its nearest-neighbor Hamiltonian, respond to one density measurement with decay laws that trade places between the topological and the trivial phase; in the cluster chain, measuring a symmetry-odd spin instead of a symmetry-even one changes the critical decay law at a fixed Hamiltonian; and with power-law couplings the width law itself changes. All decay laws and profiles are obtained in closed form.

### Paper [177]
Title: Deterministically fabricated C-band single-photon sources based on quantum dot circular Bragg grating cavities
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10988
Authors: Monica Pengerla, Yury Berdnikov, Alexander Kosarev, Dominik M. Feder, Avijit Barua, Aris Koulas-Simos, Pawe{\l} Holewa, Sven Rodt, Imad Limame, Elizaveta Semenova, Stephan Reitzenstein
Abstract: Telecom-wavelength quantum light sources are essential components for scalable fiber-based quantum networks, where on-demand emission of single photons is required for high secret key rates. Here, we demonstrate the deterministic integration of metal-organic vapor-phase epitaxy (MOVPE)-grown C-band InAs/InP quantum dots into circular Bragg grating resonators. The integrated emitters exhibit a radiative lifetime of $T_1 = (1400 \pm 50)~\mathrm{ps}$ and a spectral linewidth of $\Delta E_{\mathrm{FWHM}} = (41.0 \pm 4.0)~\mu\mathrm{eV}$. Under non-resonant excitation, we measure a post-selected single-photon purity of $(0.95 \pm 0.02)$ and a raw two-photon interference visibility of $V_{12\mathrm{ns}} = (35 \pm 2)\%$, which increases to $(70 \pm 18)\%$ under temporal post-selection. We discuss strategies to improve photon indistinguishability, including radiative-rate engineering, charge stabilization, and more selective excitation. Our results combine MOVPE growth, deterministic device integration, and telecom C-band emission, providing a route toward scalable semiconductor quantum light sources for fiber-based quantum networks.

### Paper [178]
Title: Toward universal excited-state machine-learned potentials: a local excitation Hamiltonian for size consistency and transferability
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11019
Authors: Pascal Friederich
Abstract: Universal machine-learned interatomic potentials could make excited-state simulations across chemical space routine, but excitation energies, unlike ground-state energies, are not sums of atomic contributions. The excited states of noninteracting molecules are the union of their states, which pooled multistate readouts reproduce at best for the lowest state. We introduce LEH-MACE, whose excitation energies are the eigenvalues of a local excitation Hamiltonian assembled from atom-centred blocks, so this union holds exactly. We generated TD-DFT energies and forces for about 300,000 equilibrium, thermal, and excited-state geometries of over 100,000 molecules. LEH-MACE's advantage over direct readouts grew with the distance from the training data and was largest above $S_1$, with about half their $S_2$ and $S_3$ errors for long conjugated oligomers. For molecule pairs, only LEH-MACE kept its single-molecule accuracy, while a softmin readout remained superior for $S_1$ dynamics of larger chromophores. Exact size consistency is thus one step toward such potentials.

### Paper [179]
Title: Bridging Gaps with Tensor Networks: From the Haldane Gap and Beyond
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11049
Authors: Sirui Lu
Abstract: Proofs generated by large language models are often difficult to understand, generalize, or formalize directly. Here we verify, digest, and formalize OpenAI's computer-assisted proof of a uniform gap in the periodic spin-1 Heisenberg chain using tensor-network diagrams and Lean 4. Reading the partition-function network along space and imaginary time shows how finite-ring bounds propagate across doubled lengths and inverse temperatures to all longer even rings, sharpening the spin-1 bound from the same finite inputs by a factor of 19 to $\Delta_L > 0.0729$ for all even $L \geq 36$. We identify reflection positivity as the general condition behind the proof and certify uniform gaps in seven additional spin chains and ladders. More broadly, digesting machine-generated proofs into transparent physical mechanisms yields sharper bounds, broader theorems, and reusable formal verifications.

### Paper [180]
Title: Quantum entanglement as a resource for self-organization and stabilization
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11175
Authors: Kazuki Ikeda
Abstract: Can quantum entanglement generate macroscopic order that persists after the entanglement itself disappears? We establish such a mechanism in a driven-dissipative bosonic lattice, where local entanglement enables transport that amplifies spatial fluctuations into extended Turing patterns. For the full nonlinear dynamics with noise and disorder, we derive rigorous finite-time bounds on pattern intensity for all admissible separable evolutions, allowing arbitrary classical correlations. Quantum dynamics produces pattern intensities beyond these bounds during both formation and stabilization under noise. The excess intensity also provides a lower bound on the accumulated transport contribution beyond the separable limit. We further introduce an environment that responds to emerging order by suppressing local coherence and activating classical transport. The patterns then persist as local entanglement is lost, with both stages arising from a single time-independent Lindblad equation. These results show how transient quantum resources can establish persistent macroscopic order, opening new directions for harnessing self-organization in nonequilibrium systems.

### Paper [181]
Title: A Microscopic Model Underpinning Macroscopic QED in Active Media
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11557
Authors: Daigo Oue
Abstract: We establish a microscopic foundation for macroscopic quantum electrodynamics in active media. Starting from a driven three-level system, we derive an effective two-level description with nonequilibrium stationary populations and employ a quantum central limit approach to obtain collective Langevin dynamics. This construction yields the electromagnetic susceptibility and quantum noise-current correlations from the same microscopic stationary state, providing a direct connection to the reservoir operators of conventional macroscopic quantum electrodynamics. We show that the optical response is governed by the population difference, whereas the noise correlations depend separately on the populations of the two levels. Consequently, at optical transparency, the imaginary part of the susceptibility vanishes while quantum fluctuations remain finite. We interpret the resulting noise statistics in terms of an effective reservoir occupation and a purification involving two vacuum modes, and establish a corresponding effective-temperature fluctuation--dissipation relation. Finally, we demonstrate that the residual fluctuations at transparency can induce finite heating of a nearby quantum probe.

### Paper [182]
Title: Liberation in the Heavy Limit
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11594
Authors: Sridip Pal, Gordon Rogelberg
Abstract: We study CFT four-point functions of identical scalar primaries as their dimension $\Delta$ grows for fixed $0<1$, and call them liberated if they grow at the same exponential rate as those of a generalized free field (GFF), as is the case for heavy free particles propagating along geodesics in AdS. We show that the correlator is liberated at the crossing symmetric point if and only if the light spectrum is sparse, and that liberation then holds wherever $z$ and $\bar z$ lie on the same side of $1/2$. If the low twist states are also sparse, liberation holds everywhere except in an explicit band around $z+\bar z=1$. In particular, if the primary twist gap lies above $\sqrt{2}\Delta$, then the correlator is liberated everywhere. For twist cutoffs below $\Delta$, composites of two spinning generalized free fields fail to be liberated in a region that fills the band near the double lightcone limit. In two dimensions, Virasoro primaries cannot be liberated unless the central charge grows faster than $\Delta$. The same framework sharpens the universality of large-$c$ torus partition functions of two-dimensional CFTs.

### Paper [183]
Title: Pauli propagation in adaptive local frames
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11647
Authors: Jonnathan Pineda-Jim\'enez, Mario Collura
Abstract: The cost of Pauli propagation depends on the local basis in which the evolving operator is expanded. We show that its Pauli complexity splits into an occupation part, invariant under local rotations and bounded from below by the operator mass and length distributions, and a flavour part that a suitable basis can remove. We introduce frame-adaptive Pauli propagation (FAPP), which rotates the basis of every qubit, gate by gate, to the principal axes of the operator's local polarisation, exactly and within the memory structure of Pauli propagation. This simple rule achieves most of what the best local frame can, and the polarisation predicts how much any local frame can gain. In the Ising chain in transverse and longitudinal fields, FAPP lowers the truncation error at fixed memory by up to an order of magnitude and the memory by a size-independent factor of three; on the infinite two-dimensional lattice it needs up to seven times fewer strings for a given certified accuracy, and in Clifford circuits doped with T gates two to eight times fewer.

### Paper [184]
Title: Operator Learning of Microstructure-Dependent Internal-Variable Models for Inelastic Homogenization: Approximation and Complexity
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11805
Authors: Lianghao Cao, George Stepaniants, Andrew M. Stuart
Abstract: We study the learning of homogenized constitutive laws for inelastic heterogeneous materials, aiming to replace costly concurrent unit-cell simulations with surrogates that generalize across loading histories and microstructures. We focus on the recurrent neural operator framework, motivated by the explicit form of homogenized Kelvin--Voigt linear viscoelasticity. In this framework, internal variables enable a Markovian approximation of loading-history dependence, and neural operators represent the microstructure-dependent constitutive response. For periodic Kelvin--Voigt viscoelasticity, we prove a universal approximation theorem showing that the model can approximate the homogenized constitutive law to any prescribed accuracy, uniformly in time and over a wide set of admissible strain trajectories and microstructural property fields, in two and three dimensions. This comprises a significant extension of the existing, somewhat limited, one-dimensional theory that uses explicit solutions for piecewise constant materials. Instead, in two and three dimensions, we adopt a more versatile approximation based on the spectral representation of the memory kernel that arises from homogenization. This also enables us to give an explicit bound on the internal-variable dimension, sufficient for approximation, exhibiting logarithmic growth in the inverse relative stress error and in material contrast. Numerical experiments on viscoelasticity and crystal plasticity complement the theory, illustrating the roles of internal-variable dimension and sample complexity in learning homogenized responses from unit-cell data. Theoretical results are formally verified in Lean 4.

### Paper [185]
Title: Discretising 2D Fermionic CFTs
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11807
Authors: Dazhuo Cheng, Hexuan Li, Gong Cheng, Ling-Yan Hung
Abstract: We extend the exact fixed-point tensor construction of Cheng et al. to fermionic minimal models, providing an analytic construction of fixed-point tensors for fermionic rational conformal field theories. The fermionic fixed-point tensor is defined as a correlation function of boundary-changing operators on a triangle, with careful treatment of fermionic parity and spin structure. Boundary tensors are also constructed and combined with triangle tensors to form strips, enabling the extraction of scaling dimensions for boundary-changing operators. The construction incorporates modified weights for shrinkable boundary conditions, systematic depiction of spin structures, recovering both the Neveu-Schwarz and Ramond sectors. As a benchmark, our tensor with a minimal truncation of descendant levels yields convergence to the exact bulk scaling dimensions of many low energy states for the free Majorana fermion ($c=1/2$), demonstrating the efficiency of approximate fixed point tensors from boundary changing operators. We also make use of a more general description of fermionic CFTs based on fermionisation of rational bosonic CFTs, and make a generic prescription for discretisation making use of their connections to modular tensor categories. We show that the discretised spin structures on a spin surface naturally recover the signs needed to recover the R/NS sectors. We propose that the collection of OPE coefficients of the discretised 2d CFT can be naturally interpreted as a state-sum of spin-TQFTs, analogous to the bosonic case. This work provides analytically computed starting points for tensor network simulations of fermionic critical phenomena.

### Paper [186]
Title: Crowding limits relative bacterial surface accumulation with density-induced effective heating
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11924
Authors: Yaocai Liu, Rui He, Da Wei, Longfei Li, Mingcheng Yang, Junhua Yuan, Yi Peng
Abstract: The accumulation of microswimmers near walls has been studied for decades. While hydrodynamics and single-cell kinematics are well-explored, the role of cell-cell interactions remains understudied. However, in realistic, crowded environments such as the human gut, these interactions can be just as crucial. Here, we demonstrate that they actively weaken bacterial surface accumulation. Analysis of entrapped cell tracks reveals that crowding enhances orientational fluctuations, which increase the effective rotational diffusion and facilitate wall escape. A minimal model shows that this collective effect is equivalent to raising the system's effective temperature with density.

### Paper [187]
Title: AI-Driven Discovery of Entanglement Witness in Spin Hamiltonians
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11935
Authors: Jiwei Tian, Mouyang Cheng, Mingda Li
Abstract: Entanglement is a crucial resource in quantum science and engineering, but detecting it in many-body states from experimentally accessible observables remains challenging. Here we introduce EntNet, an AI-driven framework to discover compact combinations of spin observables that accurately distinguish entangled from fully separable many-body mixed states in one-dimensional spin-$1/2$ chains governed by short-range Hamiltonians. We construct local-unitary-invariant representations of measurable Pauli correlators up to four-body order, sparsely select 16 candidate invariant feature channels, and learn their spatial structure using a Transformer architecture with rotary positional embeddings. Using only expectation values of these low-order observables, EntNet achieves $>98\%$ recall on certified entangled states with near-zero false-positive rates on separable states. Remarkably, it exhibits transferability across system sizes and distinct Hamiltonian families, including frustrated and disordered spin models, with minimal loss of accuracy. Beyond detection, EntNet provides physical insight into entanglement detection by revealing a selective spatial organization of pair correlations, while its finite-temperature decision boundary tracks the excitation gap of Jordan-Wigner quasiparticles. These results establish a transferable and physically interpretable framework for data-driven discovery of entanglement witnesses from local correlation functions.

### Paper [188]
Title: Benchmarking language models and agents for scientific reasoning in evolving materials laboratories
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12014
Authors: Xuyu Dong, Yixuan Li, Jianguo Huang, Feng Chen, Yiyun Zhou, Bo An, Zheng Liu, Da Shu
Abstract: Large language models and the multi-agent systems built around them could accelerate materials discovery by connecting scientific knowledge with experimental decisions. Existing benchmarks establish capabilities in domain knowledge, multimodal interpretation and scientific tool use. As language models increasingly support materials research workflows, their ability to distinguish closely related material states and reason accurately with quantitative evidence remains insufficiently evaluated. These abilities are essential for comparing material responses, assessing mechanisms and maintaining reliable judgements as experimental evidence accumulates. We introduce MatScope to evaluate three connected capabilities. M1 tests unaided, condition-specific reasoning through 200 tasks spanning ten research functions, using aluminium and high-entropy alloys as representative material systems. M2 tests agent-assisted transfer of related knowledge to unresolved materials questions. MatEvoBench-20 tests initial forecasts across scientific dependency chains and revisions of still-hidden responses as observations accumulate. Across eleven model deployments, including GPT-6 Astra and Claude Fable 5.1, given-input calculations and many physical explanations are handled well. Fine-grained response estimates, comparisons between similar states and quantitatively supported choices remain substantially weaker. Related literature improves selected estimates, while condition and baseline mismatches limit transfer. Progressive observations calibrate some downstream responses and destabilize earlier accurate predictions. MatScope establishes measurable targets for developing reliable scientific reasoning in future self-evolving materials laboratories and materials-aware world models.

### Paper [189]
Title: Extending the reach of real-space real-time TDDFT: new developments in the Octopus code
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12018
Authors: Nicolas Tancogne-Dejean, Martin L\"uders, Esra Ilke Albar, Heiko Appel, Franco P Bonaf\'e, Alexander Buccheri, Alessandro Casalino, Benshu Fan, Hans Fangohr, Umberto De Giovannini, Henning Glawe, Hannes H\"ubener, Simon V. B. Jensen, Christian J\"ons, Lukas Konecny, Martin Lang, Ruipeng Li, I-Te Lu, Jehferson Mello, Henri Menke, Sumit Naskar, Zhengwei Nie, Sebastian T. Ohlmann, Micael J. T. Oliveira, Subhojit Pal, Jonas Reimann, Rajarshi Sinha-Roy, Jiuyu Sun, Francesco Troisi, Angel Rubio
Abstract: Octopus is an open-source package implementing time-dependent density functional theory in a real-space, real-time formulation. Here we report on the developments since our previous contribution to the Special Topic Issue on Electronic Structure Software [J. Chem. Phys. 152, 124119 (2020)]. New exchange-correlation functionals extend the range of systems that can be treated, while advances in relativistic and all-electron methods open the door to core-electron spectroscopy. New capabilities for lattice dynamics - stress-tensor calculation, geometry optimization, support for machine-learned force fields, and phonon calculations - enable a fully ab initio description of light-induced lattice dynamics in real time. We also present developments that move beyond the dipole approximation, capturing light-matter interaction more faithfully. Improved methods for exciton dynamics complement a set of algorithmic advances - new propagation schemes, imaginary-time evolution, and a refactored grid handling more than a billion points - that together substantially improve performance and scalability. Combined with technical improvements such as AMD GPU support and a modernized build system, these developments consolidate Octopus as a versatile, high-performance platform for exploring electronic excitations, light-matter interaction, and nonequilibrium dynamics in molecules, nanostructures, and extended systems.

### Paper [190]
Title: Matrix-vector models for the 3D Ising CFT
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12071
Authors: Kaichi Mizohata, Tatsuya Seko, Asato Tsuchiya
Abstract: The construction of three-dimensional conformal field theories (3D CFTs) using interacting fermions in the lowest Landau level on a sphere has recently proven remarkably efficient, while it differs from that of conventional field theories on the fuzzy sphere. Focusing on the 3D Ising CFT, we consider a class of models that are defined by deformed Hamiltonians specified by a function which controls how the interaction is damped at high angular momenta, and this class includes the original model. We show that each model in the class can be reformulated in a basis-independent manner as a matrix--vector quantum-mechanical model, and, upon integrating out the fermionic vectors, as a matrix model. We then focus on a Gaussian choice, for which the bosonic matrices acquire a heat-kernel propagator whose width is controlled by a parameter $\xi$. Through exact diagonalization with the number of fermions up to $16$, we find that the low-energy spectra of the deformed Hamiltonians at criticality agree with those in the 3D Ising CFT for a range of $\xi$. We thus obtain matrix-model realizations of the 3D Ising CFT that, in contrast to conventional fuzzy-sphere field theories, show no sign of UV/IR mixing in the low-lying spectrum.

### Paper [191]
Title: Linear response in generalized Lotka--Volterra systems with a finite number of species
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12074
Authors: Koya Katayama, Sosuke Ito
Abstract: Understanding how species interactions shape ecosystem responses to perturbations is a central challenge in ecology. Random matrix theory (RMT) has been widely used to predict the responses of large random ecosystems, but its predictions may not accurately describe individual finite ecosystems. Here, we derive rigorous geometric bounds on the numerical ranges and spectra of Jacobian matrices that are valid for any finite number of species without assuming randomness. These bounds link long-term stability and short-term stability to the symmetric component of abundance-weighted interactions, and constrain the maximum oscillation frequency by the skew-symmetric component. Applied to random ensembles, the bounds also yield constraints on ensemble averages of long-term stability, short-term stability, and the maximum oscillation frequency in terms of standard RMT parameters. Using empirical data from marine food webs, we show that our bounds provide informative constraints on short-term stability and the maximum oscillation frequency even where RMT-based predictions fail. These results establish structural constraints on local ecosystem responses that complement RMT-based statistical predictions for large random ecosystems.

### Paper [192]
Title: Analytical Framework Relating Capacitance-Frequency Measurements and Hysteresis in Perovskite Solar Cells
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12115
Authors: Manjeet Kumar, Changzeng Ding, Mathias Nyman, Yongqi Sun, Chang-Qi Ma, Ronald \"Osterbacka, Oskar J. Sandberg
Abstract: Mobile ion migration strongly influences the electrical response and current-voltage hysteresis of perovskite solar cells. However, quantitative determination of ionic transport parameters typically requires transient measurements, extensive numerical simulations, and/or fitting procedures involving multiple uncertain parameters. Here, we present an analytical model that establishes a direct relationship between capacitance-frequency (C-f) characteristics and ionic transport properties in perovskite solar cells, while accounting for interlayer effects. The model describes the transition from the ion-screened depletion capacitance at low frequencies to the geometric capacitance at high frequencies and demonstrates that the characteristic transition frequency provides a direct measure of the dielectric relaxation time for ions. Furthermore, the model establishes a quantitative connection between C-f characteristics and scan-rate-dependent current-voltage hysteresis, enabling prediction of the scan-rate window in which maximum hysteresis occurs. Drift-diffusion simulations show excellent agreement with the analytical predictions over wide ranges of ion density, ion mobility, transport-layer mobility and doping concentration. Experimental measurements confirm the predicted correlation between capacitance and hysteresis. By providing a direct, physically transparent, and computationally efficient route for extracting ionic transport parameters from routine impedance measurements, this work offers a practical tool for quantifying ion migration and understanding dynamic phenomena in perovskite solar cells.

### Paper [193]
Title: Tunable Near-Unity Light Absorption via Surface Exciton-Polaritons in Periodic Gratings of Anisotropic Polarizable Materials
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12224
Authors: M. V. Rodrigues, M. S. Barreiro, M. I. Vasilevskiy, N. M. R. Peres
Abstract: Near-perfect light absorption in ultrathin films is hindered by two fundamental obstacles: the momentum mismatch that prevents resonant excitation of surface exciton-polaritons (SEPs) under ordinary illumination, and the 50% absorption ceiling imposed on free-standing conductive sheets by geometric symmetry. Here we show that both obstacles can be overcome simultaneously in a simple, planar geometry. A one-dimensional periodic grating of anisotropic polarizable ribbons composed of J-aggregates supporting bright molecular excitons provides the reciprocal lattice momentum needed to excite SEPs without employing an attenuated total reflection configuration, while a metallic mirror beneath a dielectric spacer folds the reflected field back onto the absorber, breaking the $50\%$ limit through interference between the incident and reflected waves. We develop a semi-analytical matrix formulation to compute the full optical response of such structure, and demonstrate that the absorptance at the first-order SEP resonance can be continuously tuned from near-unity to near-zero by varying the spacer thickness alone. The grating period and filling factor offer additional spectral control, and oblique incidence unlocks a second, out-of-plane polariton branch. These results establish periodic gratings of excitonic media as a versatile, practical and integrable platform for spectrally selective, near-perfect light absorption.

### Paper [194]
Title: Commuting-check gauging for fault-tolerant logical measurements
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12236
Authors: Zongyuan Wang
Abstract: Gauging turns a logical operator into a product of local checks, providing a route to logical measurement and lattice surgery. These checks can fail to commute outside the code space, so measuring one can randomize the outcome of another. In this work, we introduce completion qubits that make the checks commute for Clifford $\mathbb{Z}_2$ symmetries of qubit stabilizer codes. Adding one completion qubit per noncommuting pair preserves the gauged code space and its distance while keeping the checks Clifford. Completion also preserves the low-density parity-check property with a constant-factor qubit overhead. We generalize completion to qudits and establish bounds on the Clifford-hierarchy level of the completed checks in prime dimensions. Global commutation ensures that a fault-free round fixes the syndrome until the next fault and allows us to bound fault propagation independently of the number of measurement rounds. For qubit codes, we show that gauging and ungauging a distance-$d$ parent code implements a logical measurement with circuit fault distance $\Omega(d)$ in $O(d)$ rounds. We demonstrate the construction by gauging two toric codes into the $D(D_4)$ quantum double and back, with an explicit circuit whose circuit fault distance equals the parent code distance. Our results provide a framework for fault-tolerant logical measurements using commuting checks, with applications to magic-state preparation by lattice surgery.

### Paper [195]
Title: On transition probabilities for the ASEP on the half-line
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12258
Authors: Alexandr Garbali, Cengiz Gazi, Jan de Gier, William Mead, Michael Wheeler
Abstract: We study the asymmetric simple exclusion process on the half-line with particle insertion and no particle removal at the boundary. We point out that there exist two distinct multiple contour integral formulas for the transition probability between two finite particle configurations containing the same number of particles. We recall one of these formulas from the literature and prove a second, new formula by showing that it satisfies the evolution equation using a coordinate Bethe ansatz calculation and verify that the initial condition is implemented by the contour integrals. As an application we sum this formula over all final configurations and obtain an integral formula for the probability that the number of particles is conserved up to a given time. The proofs use symmetric rational functions and their properties together with contour deformations.

### Paper [196]
Title: Resetting-based designated information distribution
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12485
Authors: Xiaojuan Ma, Yan Wang, Indranil Gupta
Abstract: For a network of $n$ nodes under random gossip dynamics, we show that by introducing the additional resetting mechanism (with rate $\beta$) to a designated subset of $r$ nodes, the expected sum of all node values is asymptotically equal to $\tfrac{n}{r}$ times the sum of the initial values of the $r$ designated nodes, in the small-$\beta$ and long-time limit. We also analyze the convergence speed to this asymptotic state and provide an approximate high-probability confidence interval for the expected sum at finite time $t$ and finite $\beta$. Operationally, we propose a protocol that distributes the initial node values over the designated subset evenly (up to a prescribed precision), by appropriately turning on and off the resetting dynamics. Our work not only offers insights into classical problems such as distributed computing and network control, but also serves as a potential foundation for designing quantum algorithms that prepare desired states in quantum networks using only local operations.

### Paper [197]
Title: UniHam: A Large-Scale SOC-Complete Dataset and Benchmark for Hamiltonian Learning in Materials
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12509
Authors: Yuewen Huang, Pin Chen, Yutong Lu
Abstract: Accurate prediction of electronic Hamiltonians would enable broad property inference while avoiding the high computational cost of Density Functional Theory (DFT). However, progress toward general-purpose materials foundation models is limited by a data bottleneck: existing Hamiltonian datasets are typically small, lack structural diversity, and often omit essential relativistic physics such as spin--orbit coupling (SOC). We therefore construct UniHam, a large-scale Hamiltonian dataset and benchmark suite comprising 100,000+ DFT-computed complex-valued Hermitian Hamiltonians with full SOC, covering 72 elements and a wide range of crystal geometries and symmetries (spanning diverse lattice types and space-group families). Building on UniHam, we benchmark two representative state-of-the-art models under a standardized protocol and introduce complementary evaluation metrics that jointly assess three dimensions: (i) Hamiltonian reconstruction accuracy, (ii) out-of-distribution (OOD) generalization across composition/symmetry shifts, and (iii) the ability to support downstream property prediction from the predicted Hamiltonians. Experiments on UniHam demonstrate that the proposed benchmark and metrics effectively differentiate model capabilities, revealing intrinsic SOC- and element-dependent failure modes, large variations in compositional OOD robustness, and the necessity of spectral-level evaluation to assess whether Hamiltonian predictions reliably support downstream electronic-structure properties. Overall, UniHam provides a reproducible, SOC-complete benchmark that can sharpen model comparisons and accelerate the development of next-generation foundation models for quantum materials.

### Paper [198]
Title: Distinguishing thyroid nodule malignancy using digital palpation
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12563
Authors: Brendan M. Unikewicz, Joseph E. Bonavia, Ekaterina L. Koelliker, Abigail Klein, Katie C. Spaeth, Hannah M. Varner, S. Kiana Naghibzadeh, Yoav Horev, Sareh Parangi, Tal Cohen
Abstract: Accurate pre-operative diagnosis of thyroid nodules remains a major clinical challenge. Current diagnostic techniques rely on fine-needle aspiration and demonstrate high rates of inconclusive results, often leading to avoidable thyroidectomies with lifelong implications. Here, we introduce a mechanics-based diagnostic method that leverages Digital Palpation to determine malignancy and identify tissue subtypes in human thyroid nodules. From a cohort of patients undergoing thyroidectomies, we evaluated ex vivo thyroid specimens and processed the resulting mechanical data from 36 tests in 10 thyroid nodules. Principal Component Analysis reveals that these experimental results are distributed within a three-dimensional space of interpretable scalar features reflecting tissue grittiness, strength, and toughness, which map closely to descriptions of malignant and benign nodules given by clinicians. Our results further demonstrate that malignant nodules exhibit distinct mechanical signatures compared to their benign counterparts. This revealed a statistically significant difference (p = 9 x 10^-4) and separability (95% lower-bound Hedges' g = 1.08) between benign and malignant nodules along a direction in principal component space representing effective stiffness. Preliminary clustering patterns were also observed among malignant subtypes, including follicular, oncocytic, and papillary thyroid carcinomas. Together, these results highlight the potential of Digital Palpation as a novel mechanics-based method for tissue characterization and motivate its further development toward diagnostic applications.

### Paper [199]
Title: Ab Initio Molecular Dynamics-Guided Machine-Learning Potentials for Mapping Nitric Oxide Interactions with Oxygen-Covered Graphite
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12573
Authors: Yaideny Rodriguez Linares, Gilberto A. Alou Angulo, Samuel Del Fr\'e, Maurice Monnerville, Alejandro Rivero Santamar\'ia
Abstract: Exploratory ab initio molecular dynamics is used to construct machine-learning interatomic potentials for NO interactions with highly oriented pyrolytic graphite at 33\% bridge-site oxygen coverage. Representative configurations sampled across surface temperatures and incidence energies provide reference energies and forces for training and validation. The selected potential enables relaxation of 300 adsorption configurations and construction of spatial and angular interaction maps. These maps reveal increasingly restricted attractive regions during vertical approach and reduced short-range repulsion upon molecular reorientation. The resulting adsorption geometries and interaction landscapes guide further first-principles sampling toward refining potentials for NO oxidation dynamics.

### Paper [200]
Title: All-to-all connectivity via adiabatic ZZ interactions in a superconducting flux qubit processor
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12576
Authors: Elena Lupo, Nguyen H. Le, Eran Ginossar, Michael Stern
Abstract: Scalable superconducting quantum processors would benefit from high qubit connectivity, yet existing architectures connect neighbouring qubits via tunable couplers, which introduce substantial wiring and control overhead. Here, we propose a baseband-driven adiabatic ZZ gate that entangles any selected pair in a register of flux qubits coupled via a common cavity. The qubits stay dc-biased at their flux sweet spot. Strong drives applied selectively through local flux lines, at frequencies far below the qubit transitions, adiabatically tilt the quantization axes and generate an effective longitudinal ZZ interaction. The strength of the coupling is tunable and controlled by the amplitudes and relative phase of the drives rather than by any resonance condition. Numerical simulations give coherent gate errors below $10^{-4}$ over broad ranges of drive frequency, with comparable fidelities for two simultaneous gates in a four-qubit register. The protocol is robust against frequency fluctuations and remains effective even when many qubits are coupled to the cavity.

### Paper [201]
Title: Damped polarizability and first hyperpolarizability of large systems with hybrid functionals: self-consistent and Lanczos solvers
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12664
Authors: A. Garc\'ia-Bl\'azquez, A. Erba, A. Boccuni, J. K. Desmarais
Abstract: We present a damped coupled-perturbed Kohn-Sham method to compute the dynamical polarizability and first hyperpolarizability of finite systems, implemented in the CRYSTAL code for semi-local, global and range-separated hybrid functionals. The approach is based on the adiabatic time-dependent density functional theory for the complex-valued (hyper)polarizabilities, describing optical phenomena such as absorption, and the nonlinear second-harmonic generation, rectification and Pockels effect. These are obtained either at individual frequencies with a self-consistent solver based on Pulay extrapolation, or at an arbitrary grid of frequency and damping factors with a Lanczos solver, from which the first-order amplitudes required in the hyperpolarizability can be reconstructed. With hybrid functionals, the self-consistent solver tends to become impractical at some point beyond the absorption onset, where there are many excitations for which the external frequency lies between their energy and those of their contributing single-particle transitions, and its convergence eventually stalls for large systems. In contrast, the convergence of the Lanczos method is essentially independent of size, hence the cost of the dynamical response calculation scales with system size as the hybrid ground-state calculation. The implementation is validated in a small molecule and, as an application, we study the first and second-order optical response of a series of hexagonal boron nitride flakes of up to 324 atoms. The use of range-separated hybrid functionals allows to capture the strongly-bound first excitation of the monolayer, and evidences the failure of the self-consistent method to converge excitations with strong electron-hole attraction.

### Paper [202]
Title: One-Particle Resonances and Eigenstate Thermalization in Periodic Free-Fermion Lattices
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12701
Authors: Tristan Benoist, Vojkan Jak\v{s}i\'c, Claude-Alain Pillet, Anna Szczepanek, Cornelia Vogel
Abstract: We study eigenstate fluctuations of the particle number in the nearest-neighbour free-fermion model on the periodic cubic lattice $(\mathbb{Z}/L\mathbb{Z})^d$. For every fixed dimension, every particle number, every subvolume, and every normalized energy eigenstate, we prove a bound of order $NL^{d-1}$ for the particle-number variance. The corresponding eigenstate-thermalization estimate is of order $L^{d-1}/(N\eta^2)$ for fluctuations of relative size $\eta$. In particular, at positive particle density the bound decays as the inverse linear size. The proof decomposes the centered particle-number operator according to one-particle energy transfer and treats the zero- and nonzero-transfer components separately. The nonzero-transfer part is controlled by a sharp resonance bound, proved from Mann's theorem on relations among roots of unity, while the zero-transfer part is estimated by a Hilbert-Schmidt argument. These bounds are optimal in every dimension: the maximal nonzero-gap multiplicity has order $L^{d-1}$, and suitable many-body eigenstates realize matching variance lower bounds and show that the concentration bound cannot be improved in estimates uniform in the tolerance parameter.

### Paper [203]
Title: Global Asymptotics of a Toda Soliton Condensate with Random Scattering Data
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12748
Authors: Guido Mazzuca, Mateusz Piorkowski
Abstract: We study a dense $N$-soliton condensate for the infinite Toda lattice in the limit $N \to \infty$. In contrast to previous mathematical studies of soliton gases where the norming constants are assumed to be deterministic or infinitesimal, we consider initial scattering data where both the discrete eigenvalues and the norming constants are independent random variables. The norming constants $\gamma_j$ are allowed to be arbitrarily large under a mild moment condition $\mathbb{E}[\gamma_j^\nu] < \infty$ ($\nu > 1$). Our main result is divided into two parts. First, we develop a probabilistic Riemann--Hilbert framework to show that the system self-averages: despite large, independent fluctuations in the initial data, the random Toda lattice variables converge deterministically to an effective continuum model at a polynomial rate $\mathcal{O}(N^{-\delta})$ for $0 < \delta < 1/2$. Second, by performing the Deift--Zhou nonlinear steepest descent analysis of the continuum problem, we obtain the complete leading-order macroscopic description of the lattice across the entire space-time plane. The solution decomposes into five distinct finite-gap regimes, characterized explicitly by modulated elliptic and genus-2 waves with an error of order $\mathcal{O}(1/\ln N)$. In contrast to the straight characteristic rays typical of standard soliton gas limits, the interfaces separating these five macroscopic regimes in the space-time plane are genuinely non-linear curves; in particular, the boundary of the modulated genus-2 region is completely non-linear. Finally, under our assumptions, we establish the Nonlinear Dispersion Relations for the Toda soliton condensate and prove that the macroscopic ensemble averages are determined by the soliton gas density of states.

### Paper [204]
Title: An efficient classical algorithm for OTOCs in chaotic quantum dynamics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12773
Authors: Yunchao Liu, Yihui Quek, Shreya Vardhan, Tianci Zhou
Abstract: Estimating out-of-time-ordered correlators (OTOCs) in individual instances of random quantum circuits has been proposed as a candidate for verifiable quantum advantage, and demonstrated to be hard for conventional classical algorithms such as tensor networks and Monte Carlo. Using insights from quantum chaos, we develop and implement a polynomial-time classical algorithm for the four-point ${\rm OTOC}^{(1)}$ and the eight-point ${\rm OTOC}^{(2)}$ in typical instances of one-dimensional (1D) quantum circuits. Our results demonstrate average-case classical tractability of estimating OTOCs in the sensitive regime where circuit-to-circuit fluctuations are large. Our algorithm organizes the OTOC into a rapidly converging series, where the leading term includes contributions from histories within permutation degrees of freedom in the replicated Hilbert space, and subleading terms arise from excursions outside this space. Although chaos is often viewed as an insurmountable barrier to classical simulation, our algorithm exploits a form of dephasing caused by chaos, which suppresses the subleading terms. We provide strong theoretical and numerical evidence to support this suppression. Further, we demonstrate that practical implementations of our algorithm estimate ${\rm OTOC}^{(1)}$ and ${\rm OTOC}^{(2)}$ in 1D circuits with high accuracy, achieving higher signal-to-noise ratio at larger circuit depths compared to recent 2D experiments by Google. Extending our algorithm to higher dimensions, and resolving the status of quantum advantage in 2D OTOCs, remain open problems.

### Paper [205]
Title: Property F implies weak integrality for non-degenerate pivotal braided fusion categories
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12836
Authors: Justin Asher, Jonah Berean-Dutcher, Clay Cordova, Julio Parra-Martinez, Richard Tuddenham
Abstract: We prove that every non-degenerate pivotal braided fusion category with property F is pseudo-unitary and weakly integral. In particular, every modular category with property F is weakly integral. Neither sphericality nor pseudo-unitarity is assumed. Passing to the Drinfeld centre extends the result to degenerate categories whose dimensions are positive on the intersection of the M\"uger centre with the adjoint subcategory; these include every braided fusion category with a pseudo-unitary Galois conjugate. Physically, the result connects the arithmetic of topological orders to the computational power of anyon braiding: non-integer squared total quantum dimension guarantees infinite braid group images, a necessary condition for universal quantum computation.

### Paper [206]
Title: Scaling at quantum phase transitions above the upper critical dimension
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.08081
Authors: A. Langheld, J. A. Koziol, P. Adelhardt, S. C. Kapfer, K. P. Schmidt
Abstract: The hyperscaling relation and standard finite-size scaling (FSS) are known to break down above the upper critical dimension due to dangerous irrelevant variables. We establish a coherent formalism for FSS at quantum phase transitions above the upper critical dimension following the recently introduced Q-FSS formalism for thermal phase transitions. Contrary to long-standing belief, the correlation sector is affected by dangerous irrelevant variables. The presented formalism recovers a generalized hyperscaling relation and FSS form. Using this new FSS formalism, we determine the full set of critical exponents for the long-range transverse-field Ising chain in all criticality regimes ranging from the nearest-neighbor to the long-range mean field regime. For the same model, we also explicitly confirm the effect of dangerous irrelevant variables on the characteristic length scale.

### Paper [207]
Title: Shaking the causal tree: On the faithfulness and minimality assumptions beyond pairwise interactions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.10665
Authors: Tiago Martinelli, Diogo O. Soares-Pinto, Francisco A. Rodrigues
Abstract: Higher-order mechanisms can make genuine causes invisible to causal discovery, but whether this reflects fine tuning or a structural limitation remains unclear. We prove, for a binary causal family, that the failure of faithfulness is not a measure-zero pathology: it persists for every nonzero coupling vector, even though every active edge remains causally minimal. Experiments with established causal-discovery methods show how this separation propagates into algorithm-dependent failures.
