For the standard local walk on a $k$-CNF with an assignment satisfying a majority of each clause, we find the sharp source threshold $s_*$: below it, on some instances one admissible source forces nontermination for every clause selection; at it, with fixed $D$, the expected number of flips is at most cubic in the initial distance, against linear under independence, with matching instances at distances up to a constant fraction of the number of variables; a fixed margin above it restores linear dependence. For noisy predecessor search with prefix-bounded comparison errors, verified backtracking attains any prescribed error probability at or above a source threshold whose sharpness we leave open, with the same cubic-to-linear query bounds. Restarting on the shared tape restores exponential tails exactly when the nontermination event has a prefix cover of cost below one.

### Paper [312]
Title: Krylov complexity and spectral density of BMN matrix model
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24632
Authors: Dibakar Roychowdhury
Abstract: We use Krylov complexity as a typical diagnostic of quantum many body dynamics in the context of BMN matrix model at large mass gap. We calculate several physical entities, for example, the moments and return amplitudes at large mass deformation. We calculate them for both spread complexity of states as well as Krylov growth of operators in the matrix model. We propose a general expression for the moments at any given order $n$. We also discuss orthogonal polynomials for spread as well as operator Krylov complexity. In the context of the Krylov operator growth we further compute the spectral function and the density of states. A careful analysis of the spectral function near the resonance reveals various IR divergences in addition to the physical collective modes. These collective modes appear to be exceptionally stable due to large mass gap. On the other hand, the UV of the theory appears to be extremely damped due to higher scattering rates. We carefully diagnose these IR divergences near resonance which reveals that these collective modes are in fact non-propagating and should be thought of as diffusion modes or relaxation modes with infinite relaxation time. We also calculate the Krylov variance and Krylov entropy for the matrix model and in particular at early time. The linear early time growth of the Krylov entropy confirms the onset of quantum chaos in the matrix model at large mass gap.

### Paper [313]
Title: Landau Levels on the Surface of a Cube
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25684
Authors: Almila Kura, Bayram Tekin, Mehmet \"Ozg\"ur Oktel
Abstract: We study the quantum mechanics of a charged particle confined to the surface of a cube enclosing a magnetic monopole. The magnetic field is chosen to have a constant magnitude on each face and to point along the outward normal, preserving the rotational symmetry of the cube. We formulate the continuum problem using two gauge patches on the cube surface and show that consistency of the wavefunction gives the Dirac quantization condition. Since an explicit vector potential does not remain invariant under ordinary rotations, we construct gauge-modified rotation operators and use them to classify the eigenstates. Even monopole charges are described by the irreducible representations of the cubic rotation group O, while odd monopole charges require the spinorial representations of the binary octahedral group 2O. We compute the spectrum with a gauge-covariant finite-difference discretization and find Landau-level-like manifolds whose degeneracies are split by the discrete cubic symmetry. We also study the corresponding tight-binding Hofstadter problem on the discretized cube. The resulting spectrum contains the usual magnetic subband structure together with additional gap states localized near the cube corners.

### Paper [314]
Title: Vibe-FDTR: An agent-oriented framework for reproducible frequency-domain thermoreflectance data analysis
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28200
Authors: Fuwei Yang, Weiheng Li, Bai Song
Abstract: Frequency-domain thermoreflectance (FDTR) is a laser pump-probe technique widely used to measure thermal properties at the micro- and nanoscale; however, it relies on a complex data analysis procedure that demands substantial domain expertise and is susceptible to subtle human errors. Here, we present Vibe-FDTR, an agent-oriented framework that enables large language model (LLM) agents to perform reliable and reproducible FDTR analyses directly from natural language requests. This framework couples a configuration-driven FDTR code package, which enforces physical and parametric consistency, with procedural agent skills that translate user intentions into organized and verifiable analysis steps. We evaluate Vibe-FDTR using a controlled benchmark with two levels: synthetic single-step tasks and real-data multi-step tasks based on measurements of gold-coated graphite samples. Across the two levels, agents using Vibe-FDTR achieve success rates of 100% and 98.9%, respectively. In sharp contrast, ablating skills (Code-agent) reduces performance to 91.4% and 36.7%, which drops further to 38.6% and 0% when the domain package is also omitted (Agent-only). Beyond success rate, Vibe-FDTR also reduces computational cost by 87.7% relative to the Code-agent variant and cuts execution time by more than 60%. Finally, an optional expert mode supports experimental planning via autonomous sensitivity and uncertainty evaluations, and formulates physically grounded recommendations for underspecified tasks. These results demonstrate that encapsulating domain code and expert knowledge into agent skills offers a promising route toward low-barrier, autonomous, and trustworthy thermal metrology.

### Paper [315]
Title: Critical and multicritical Kasner scaling in holographic phase transitions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04522
Authors: Ling-Long Gao, Yan Liu, Hong-Da Lyu
Abstract: We study how holographic phase transitions imprint their scaling laws on the local Kasner geometry inside Einstein-scalar black holes. At fixed double-trace coupling, we derive the near-critical scaling of the deviation of the first-epoch Kasner exponent from the Schwarzschild value: $p_t^{(1)}+\frac{1}{3} \propto O^2 \propto (T_c-T)^{1/r}$, where $O$ denotes the boundary condensate and $r$ labels ordinary criticality ($r=1$), tricriticality ($r=2$), and higher multicriticality ($r\geq3$). A super-exponential scalar potential generates a sequence of Kasner epochs separated by scalar-field bounces. We further find that any later epoch that can be tracked continuously across the transition inherits the same temperature exponent, while its coefficient depends on the epoch. Numerical solutions confirm these predictions for the ordinary and tricritical cases. Finally, we show analytically that the leading irrelevant exponent of the infrared fixed point governs the low-temperature Kasner scaling, and verify the resulting scaling numerically for the first Kasner exponent.

### Paper [316]
Title: Multiphysics simulation of all-optical switching and sequential magneto-optic readout in an integrated Si$_3$N$_4$/GdFeCo waveguide
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22969
Authors: Muhammad Arief Mulyana, Budi Adiperdana, Ayi Bahtiar
Abstract: Femtosecond pulses guided in a silicon nitride waveguide have recently switched a Co/Gd magnetic element patterned on top of it, a step towards magnetic memory built into photonic circuits. The guided mode heats such an element non-uniformly, and, to our knowledge, no calculation has yet followed the written state from this heating to an optical readout. Here we model both steps for an 8 nm GdFeCo film inserted across a Si$_3$N$_4$ waveguide. A three-dimensional finite-difference time-domain calculation gives the absorbed pump energy. This energy drives a four-temperature model and calibrated stochastic Landau-Lifshitz-Bloch dynamics, and a later probe pulse reads the recovered state. At the reference fluence, a single pump pulse reverses 86% of the film from either saturated state, and this fraction changes little over a 2.4-fold range of incident fluence. The unswitched cells lie where the absorbed fluence stays below the single-cell threshold, and a threshold rule applied to the absorption map reproduces the full calculation. When the film starts from zero magnetization, thermal noise sets the direction of each recovered local moment at random. Between the saturated and random states, the probe energy fractions change by at most 0.02. The magnetic Faraday and Kerr rotations are far more sensitive and become about 12 and 130 times smaller, respectively, in the random state than in a saturated film. The reflected signal also contains a magnetization-independent geometric background, which can be removed by subtracting a reference signal. The model therefore predicts that a guided probe can distinguish the written states through its polarization. It assumes a homogeneous film and omits heat flow and exchange between neighbouring cells.

### Paper [317]
Title: Physical policy gradient theorem for in situ stochastic-adjoint training
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05808
Authors: William Tuxbury, Zin Lin
Abstract: In situ adjoint training extracts parameter gradients directly from measurement, but has so far been limited to reciprocal or restricted systems. We introduce the physical counterpart of the policy gradient theorem: a stochastic-adjoint gradient estimator that lifts these constraints by trading reciprocity for nondegenerate diffusion. As validation, we train a nonlinear resonator network, whose own dynamics supply the policy, against antagonistic temporal modulations with gradients from measured stochastic trajectories alone, without finite differences or a separate adjoint experiment.

### Paper [318]
Title: Topology Obstructs Pure Foundation Neural Quantum States
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07591
Authors: Timothy Heightman, Elena Orlova, Philip Mantrov, Aleksei Ustimenko
Abstract: Foundation models for ground states in spin-1/2 systems are a promising method for problems ranging from quantum chemistry to identifying new phase diagrams. Nearly all such models are currently pure-states that condition on the Hamiltonian's parameters, whose Monte Carlo samples give energy estimates according to the variational principle. In this contribution, we show that this representation is topologically obstructed. For any gapped Hamiltonian family whose ground-state bundle is non-trivial, every continuous normalized state-vector model has zero fidelity with the ground state at some parameter value in the Hamiltonian family. For that value, the energy is at least one spectral gap, $\Delta$, with an $O(\Delta)$ gap in an open-neighbourhood of that point. We show that this is a sufficient no-go also in the case of degenerate ground-state manifolds, time dynamics, and periodic systems with mixed space-time topology, demonstrating these obstructions on one- and two-qubit systems. We discuss how this causes a spike in the fidelity susceptibility, giving a numerical signature of a phase-transition where there is none. Finally, we show that operator-valued models canonically avoid these obstructions and preserve topological information, implying a structural necessity in representation for foundation neural quantum states.

### Paper [319]
Title: Exact Operator Complexity Measures from Random Matchgate Unitaries
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38300
Authors: Gregory A. L. White, Jens Eisert, Neil Dowling
Abstract: Matchgate circuits describe classically tractable dynamics in a physically relevant setting while nonetheless exhibiting features characteristic of complex quantum systems, such as high amounts of magic. This invites the broader question: to what extent are typical complexity measures saturated under trivial dynamics? Here, we address this by studying the behaviour of different Heisenberg-picture complexity measures in random matchgate ensembles. Our main technical contribution is a tractable and generalisible free-fermionic unitary Weingarten calculus formulated entirely in the Majorana basis. This method is tailored to computing moments of operators, and simplifies to a combinatorics problem which depends on the boundary conditions prescribed by a given operator functional. Using this framework, we derive closed-form expressions for the typical local-operator entanglement, operator stabiliser entropy, and higher-order out-of-time-ordered correlators. For extensive initial Majorana strings, both operator entanglement and stabiliser entropy obey volume laws, even though the underlying circuits remain classically simulable. Out-of-time-ordered correlators can likewise become exponentially small, showing that their late-time saturation value alone does not witness computational hardness and constituting a classically tractable example of emergent free independence. Finally, we relate these separations to operator resource theories, showing how operator entanglement beyond the Gaussian baseline lower-bounds the number of non-Gaussian gates in doped matchgate circuits. Our results present a practical toolbox for computing exact averages over random matchgates, while clarifying the distinction between quantum resources versus simulation complexity, and their dependence on the representation being exploited.

### Paper [320]
Title: Optimal Scaling of Unitary Design Formation in $U(1)$-Symmetric Random Circuits: A Bottleneck Slower than Charge Transport
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39458
Authors: Toshihiro Yada
Abstract: Understanding randomness formation under conservation laws is important both for characterizing chaotic phenomena in isolated quantum systems and for symmetry-constrained quantum information processing. $U(1)$-symmetric random circuits on an $N$-qubit system provide a minimal tractable setting for this problem, in which the formation rate of unitary designs was conjectured to be governed by charge transport in the underlying circuit geometry. However, whether charge transport indeed sets the slowest relaxation mode has remained an open problem, due to the difficulty in deriving a tight lower bound on the symmetric design formation rate. In this work, we resolve this problem by determining the optimal scaling of the unitary $k$-design formation rate for $k\leq O(\sqrt{\log N})$ across a wide range of circuit geometries, including lattices, expander graphs, and all-to-all interactions. In particular, for $k\geq 2$, we find that the formation rate is governed by a two-particle encounter rate---the rate at which two random walkers encounter each other in a given circuit geometry. This encounter rate is parametrically smaller than the single-particle transport rate in many geometries, thereby disproving the previous conjecture. We further establish that this encounter mechanism indeed sets the design formation rate by deriving its matching lower bound with a novel proof technique based on an auxiliary subgroup ensemble. Although this encounter mode imposes a robust bottleneck on design formation in $U(1)$-symmetric random circuits, we also show that this bottleneck can be circumvented by using symmetry-breaking local gates and provide an explicit protocol that achieves faster design formation. These results establish a new picture of randomness formation under conservation laws and provide guiding principles for characterizing and efficiently generating symmetry-constrained randomness.

### Paper [321]
Title: A No-Go Theorem for Order-Two Clifford Electric-Magnetic Duality
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02097
Authors: Shunta Takahashi, Zhi Li, Beni Yoshida
Abstract: Electromagnetic duality in the toric code has order two at the level of anyon types and acts as a Hadamard-type logical transformation on the encoded quantum information. Here, we ask whether it can likewise be realized microscopically as an order-two Clifford operation. For the $\mathbb{Z}_2$ toric code, we prove that any locality-preserving Clifford realization of electric-magnetic exchange cannot have order two. Our proof is fully general and requires neither translation symmetry with restricted families of system sizes nor a fixed pairing between vertex and plaquette stabilizers. Geometrically, the proof locally emulates the unavoidable crossing between electric and magnetic strings, similar to a cross-cap in a non-orientable manifold. Extending the problem to the $\mathbb{Z}_N$ toric code, we find that an order-two Clifford realization exists for odd $N$, whereas it is impossible for even $N$. Our result shows that the order-two exchange of closed-string operators cannot be extended to an order-two exchange of open-string operators.

### Paper [322]
Title: Singlet sector of the vector model with angular potential
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09815
Authors: Srijan Kumar
Abstract: We study the singlet sector of large-$N$, $O(N)$ vector model on $S^1_\beta\times S^2_r$ in presence of an angular potential $\Omega$. We compute the free energy of the model both at the free fixed point as well as the non-trivial fixed point. The model undergoes a phase transition when the temperature $T$ and angular potential attain the relation $T^2r^2\sim N (1-\omega^2)$, where $\omega=r\Omega$ and $r$ is the radius of $S^2$, with $Tr\gg1$, at both the fixed points, with different numerical coefficients. The transition was studied earlier at zero angular potential at $Tr\sim \sqrt{N}$. The holonomy eigenvalue distribution, having support on the full circle below the transition, develops a gap above it, called the Gross-Witten-Wadia transition. The angular potential introduces an additional parameter to such a transition, tuning it, the transition can occur even at a fixed temperature. We show that for the free model transition can occur as $1-\omega^2\sim 1/N$ while $Tr\sim O(1)$. For the interacting model the computation is performed at a limit $Tr\gg1$, thus we show the same at a large but fixed temperature which does not scale as $\sqrt{N}$. In the ungapped phase, the free energy exhibits a double pole near $\omega^2=1$ for both the fixed points. This is possibly due to the fact that the theory projected onto the singlets is non-local and therefore need not obey the leading simple pole scaling of a local thermal effective action.

### Paper [323]
Title: $q$-deformed Weil-Petersson volumes from the spectral edge of DSSYK and supersymmetric extensions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13899
Authors: Wasif Ahmed, Joydeep Naskar, Krishan Saraswat
Abstract: The $q$-deformed Weil-Petersson volumes of Do and Norbury are the top-degree parts of Okuyama's discrete volumes, which are defined by topological recursion on the spectral curve of the double-scaled SYK (DSSYK) model. We show that the same $q$-deformed volumes can also be obtained from a double-scaled matrix model whose disk density is found by zooming in on the soft edges of the DSSYK density. Applying the same procedure to the hard edge of $\mathcal{N}=1$ DSSYK, we obtain candidate $q$-deformed $\mathcal{N}=1$ super Weil-Petersson volumes. They are polynomials of fixed degree, and they reduce to $\mathcal{N}=1$ super Weil-Petersson volumes in a scaled $q\to1$ limit. These candidate volumes differ from the $\mathcal{N}=1$ volumes of Do and Norbury in (arXiv:2510.12431) up to what we call ``irrelevant'' contributions which exactly vanish in the scaled $q\to 1$ limit. Together, these results demonstrate how $q$-deformed volumes can be naturally associated to the spectral edges of various DSSYK models and provides an intuitive way to construct them in supersymmetric contexts.

### Paper [324]
Title: Imaging the topological sign of a Dirac mass with spin-polarized tunneling
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17464
Authors: Payman Kazemikhah, Ruolun Zhang, Byunghoon Kim, Xiangyu Luo, Zachary Ducorsky, Yu-Peng Zhu, Md. Shafayat Hossain, M. Zahid Hasan
Abstract: A Dirac mass is characterized not only by its magnitude but also by its sign, which influences the Berry curvature, Hall response, and the propagation direction of chiral boundary modes. However, local spectroscopy of magnetic topological surfaces has so far accessed only the gap magnitude $|M|$. Here we show how the topological sign of $M$ can be determined locally. We first establish the sign information that spin-resolved tunneling can access and the reference required to obtain an absolute Hall sign. Although spin--orbital entanglement generally obscures the relation between measured spin and Dirac pseudospin, crystalline rotation symmetry alleviates this ambiguity at the band edge. On a $C_3$ axis, an axial tip couples to a fully spin-polarized state whose tunneling asymmetry directly reveals the sign of the occupied-band Berry curvature, independent of orbital composition. We further show that the zeroth Landau level provides an independent mass-sign measurement with an unpolarized tip, while opposite domains and their chiral domain-wall modes provide self-consistency checks. These results pave the way for imaging the locations where the topological character changes within a material, rather than merely identifying where a spectral gap appears.

### Paper [325]
Title: Exact holographic superfluid at the Bogomolny point: transport, large charge, and R\'enyi entropies
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40371
Authors: Siqing Yu
Abstract: The probe-limit Abelian Higgs model in AdS$_5$ is a standard holographic model of a strongly coupled superfluid, usually solved numerically. With the scalar mass at the Breitenlohner-Freedman bound and the quartic coupling at the Bogomolny value, its condensed phase has a closed form on every static, uncharged AdS$_5$ background with maximally symmetric spatial sections. We prove that it is the global minimum of the free energy at fixed charge, and linearly stable, as is any nodeless condensate of the model in standard quantization with nonnegative quartic coupling and no superflow. On the planar black brane it is the superfluid at all temperatures below $T_c$: the superfluid fraction follows the Gorter-Casimir two-fluid law exactly, and the speed and attenuation of sound are in closed form. In global AdS$_5$ it is the lowest state of charge $Q$ on $\mathbb{R}\times S^3$ within the model, with its scaling dimension in closed form at all $Q$, classically in the bulk. Its excitations include towers, from an infrared fixed point of the probe sector, that the superfluid effective theory does not describe. On the hyperbolic black holes that compute charged R\'enyi entropies of a ball, it is a homogeneous hairy phase whose condensate does not fall off toward the entangling surface. With a pinning field there, the phase sets in at R\'enyi index exactly one at zero chemical potential, as found for a neutral scalar. Well below a bound on the quartic coupling, the static, homogeneous solutions computed at other couplings and masses keep an infrared fixed point with towers, but not the exact two-fluid law.

### Paper [326]
Title: Dysfunctional Functionals and their Therapies in Time-Dependent Density Functional Theory
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00377
Authors: Neepa T. Maitra
Abstract: The exchange-correlation functional in Time-Dependent Density Functional Theory (TDDFT) is known to have dependence on past densities and initial states, and yet this memory-dependence is ignored in practical calculations which almost always use an adiabatic approximation. We discuss the dysfunction that arises due to this deficiency and point to therapies that go beyond the adiabatic approximation. Still, adiabatic TDDFT has had success in a wide range of systems for response properties and for fully non-equilibrium dynamics, and why this is so is only partially understood. This, together with the rigorous foundation of the exact theory and its favorable system-size scaling, provides tremendous impetus for continuing to strive towards improved approximations to the exact functionals, and a deeper understanding of the impact of approximations in applications.

### Paper [327]
Title: Autonomous Analysis of Thermoelectric Materials Big Data by Multi-Agentic AI and Construction of a Knowledge Base and Specialized AI
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00627
Authors: Yusuke Hashimoto, Takaaki Tomai
Abstract: Large databases of paper-derived experimental materials data hide essential information on materials. Their analysis is demanding, large-scale and complex, making manual knowledge extraction very difficult. To address this challenge, we developed Multi-Agentic AI (MAA) for automated, autonomous analysis of materials experimental big data and a specialized AI built on the obtained knowledge. MAA's nine role-specific AI agents, under predefined instructions and quality checks, autonomously perform data extraction, compositional feature generation, machine-learning modeling, feature-importance analysis and knowledge-base recording, iterating while inheriting previous knowledge. Delegating numerical processing to code makes large-scale data tractable; recording every choice and result enables later tracing to sources. In four thermoelectric-data experiments varying theme and cycle counts, the knowledge graph grew: knowledge clustered by theme and vocabulary was shared across themes. The specialized AI, searching and reading these records, answered materials questions citing sources. Retrospective verification also revealed errors, traced through the records to their sources.

### Paper [328]
Title: Symmetry breaking from conformality loss on holographic R\'enyi defects
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03413
Authors: Siqing Yu
Abstract: The R\'enyi entropies of a ball are computed by the R\'enyi defect (the twist operator viewed as a conformal defect), which in holography is dual to a hyperbolic black hole. We identify hyperbolic black holes with homogeneous charged scalar hair as conformal defects that break the U(1) symmetry. At large $N$ and for repulsive self-interactions, conformality loss of the R\'enyi defect turns explicit symmetry breaking into spontaneous breaking: below a critical index $n_*$ the symmetry-breaking defect is reached only with an explicit source on the entangling surface; at $n_*$ the dimension of a defect operator from a light charged scalar turns complex; beyond $n_*$ the R\'enyi defect flows to the symmetry-breaking defect with no source. The condensate, expected to break the symmetries of hyperbolic space, instead preserves them outside a layer at the entangling surface, whose width diverges at $n_*$ with Berezinskii-Kosterlitz-Thouless scaling. The universal term of the R\'enyi entropy therefore jumps at $n_*$ to that of the symmetry-breaking defect. We demonstrate this mechanism in the probe Abelian Higgs model in AdS$_5$ at the Bogomolny point, with closed-form saddles and defect data, and numerical flows. For a ball we prove that a positive source-free flow exists exactly when $n>n_*$ and the radius is large enough, and that it ends at the symmetry-breaking defect as the radius grows.

### Paper [329]
Title: Towards Multiscale Molecular Representation Learning through Geometry-Based Fragmentation for Polymer Property Prediction
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07998
Authors: Makoto Takamoto, Chaitanya Sampara, Viktor Zaverkin, Daniel O\~noro-Rubio, Mathias Niepert
Abstract: It is well established that molecular substructures can contribute unequally to chemical and physical properties, with specific motifs or side chains often playing dominant roles. Fragment-based representations have therefore emerged as an effective strategy for incorporating chemically meaningful inductive biases, as exemplified by molecular fingerprints and fragment-based attention mechanisms. However, many existing fragmentation-based machine learning methods rely on predefined chemical rules, which may yield suboptimal fragmentation for previously unseen or chemically unfamiliar structures and often overlook the importance of multiscale molecular representations. To address these limitations, we formulate molecular fragmentation as a radius-constrained optimization problem inspired by the k-center formulation, where the number of clusters is not fixed a priori but emerges from the specified radius, and propose a geometry-based fragmentation method that partitions arbitrary molecular or material structures using largely geometric information, without relying on predefined chemical fragmentation rules. The method provides flexible control over fragment size, enabling the construction of multiscale fragment representations for scale-adaptive modeling. Building on this framework, we develop a geometric fragment-based attention mechanism that integrates multiscale structural information and incorporate it into a multimodal machine learning model combining p-SMILES representations with three-dimensional (3D) conformational information. Experiments on a benchmark polymer dataset show that incorporating 3D conformational information improves prediction accuracy for several properties over models based exclusively on p-SMILES representations. Explainable artificial intelligence analyses further provide insights into the physical relevance of the learned representations.

### Paper [330]
Title: Structure is in the zoom: probing neural symmetry through dimensionality scaling
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08506
Authors: Adil Amin
Abstract: Scaling exponents of neural dimensionality are read as properties of the code, and used to compare representations and benchmark models against cortex. Yet one recording of 11,311 mouse V1 neurons gives 0.25, 0.30 or 0.35, depending only on how it is subsampled. We show that such an exponent measures the population and the probe together. We split it exactly into a floor, which random subsets of the same sizes give, and a shift, which a declared axis of stimulus classes adds. The usual random-subsample exponent is almost all floor. Stimulus direction lifts the exponent in every grating recording by reading both symmetry sectors of the code: orientation, which repeats every half turn, and direction, which does not. The lift lives in trial-to-trial fluctuations that differ between directions, while the class means, which linear decoders read, pull it down. Two measured terms, how much the classes' fluctuations overlap and how the means sit against them, account for the shift. They give its sign where it changes: in Allen Neuropixels recordings, whose class means mostly raise the exponent, and in macaque V1. In an XY lattice whose classes stay on one orbit, temperature flips the sign as the two terms say, and in GPT-2 the shift ranks the indirect-object circuit's name-mover and backup heads. Reported with its path, its floor and its shift, an exponent says how much structure a probe finds and what carries it. Our open tool, rung, reports all three for any labelled neural recording or language-model layer: https://github.com/adilamin89/structure-in-the-zoom
